# Market research — declarative desktop-action executors (2025–2026)

Compiled 2026-07-28 from live web research. Sources inline. Re-verify
claims (maintenance states, pricing, portal adoption) before quoting
them anywhere public — this is design input, not published fact.

{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Deep web research task (use WebSearch/WebFetch extensively; current date July 2026). I'm designing \"pixelactions\" — a planned universal, cross-platform (macOS/Windows/Linux incl. Wayland) CLI tool in Rust that EXECUTES desktop interactions (click, type, drag, scroll, key chords) from declarative files, consuming coordinate ground-truth JSON produced by a sister tool (pixelcoords, which freezes the screen, lets humans mark labeled regions, and outputs pixel-exact coordinates + crops + template-based re-location + point verification with exit codes).\n\nResearch the CURRENT market state (2025-2026) and report:\n\n1. **Existing action/input-automation tools per platform and cross-platform**: AutoHotkey (v2 state, Windows-only?), Hammerspoon/Keyboard Maestro/cliclick (macOS), xdotool/ydotool/dotool/wtype (Linux, X11 vs Wayland), pyautogui/pynput (Python, cross-platform pains), robotjs (dead?), nut.js (state/licensing 2026?), SikuliX→OculiX, Selenium/Playwright (browser-only boundary), Windows Power Automate Desktop, RPA suites (UiPath/Automation Anywhere — enterprise, heavy). For each: platform coverage, maintenance status, licensing/price, and their biggest user complaints.\n2. **The gap**: is there ANY maintained, cross-platform, single-binary CLI that executes desktop input from declarative files with verification? What do people currently cobble together (search reddit/HN/SO for \"cross platform pyautogui alternative\", \"ydotool wayland automation\", \"desktop automation cli 2025/2026\" etc.)?\n3. **Computer-use AI agents as a market force**: how do Anthropic computer use, OpenAI Operator, and open-source agent frameworks currently EXECUTE clicks (their own injectors? pyautogui? cloud VMs?)? Is there demand for a deterministic, verifiable local action executor that agents call as a tool (search for MCP servers for desktop control, \"computer use MCP\", agent action executors)?\n4. **Adjacent validation**: GUI test automation for desktop apps (Appium desktop drivers, WinAppDriver status, dogtail/AT-SPI) — where does coordinate-based testing still matter vs accessibility-tree automation, and what are the known limits of a11y-tree approaches (games, canvas apps, Electron quirks, remote desktops/VMs)?\n5. **Positioning white space**: given a sister tool that produces human-verified labeled coordinates with self-healing (template re-location) and point asserts, what would make a declarative executor legitimately valuable vs \"just use pyautogui\"? What niches are underserved (Wayland users, CI for desktop apps, computer-use agent guardrails, kiosk/QA rigs)?\n\nReturn a dense, sourced report (markdown, with URLs inline) — tool-by-tool table where useful, then the gap analysis and positioning conclusions. Be honest about where the idea is weak or already served."},"uuid":"d450a254-e6d6-49c4-9596-561b029337d7","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:37:56.910Z","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"01027bcd-e32a-47a2-ab49-607e38bcc4ab","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- sentry:sentry-debug-issue: Debug and fix a Sentry issue — find it (by link, ID, or search), pull full context (stack trace, breadcrumbs, trace, logs), optionally run Seer root-cause / autofix, apply the code fix, and resolve it via a `Fixes PROJECT-NAME-12A` commit/PR. Use when working a known error or hunting one down to fix.\n- sentry:sentry-feature-setup: Configure specific Sentry features beyond basic SDK setup. Use when asked to monitor AI/LLM calls, set up OpenTelemetry pipelines, create alerts and notifications, or set up Sentry Snapshots.\n- sentry:sentry-get-started: Guided entry point for using Sentry through your agent. Orients you to your current setup and, for a new project, sets up Sentry end to end with sane defaults — provision a project, install the SDK (errors, tracing, and whatever it enables by default), and confirm real telemetry reaches Sentry. Routes other intents (adding more signals, fixing issues) to the right skill.\n- sentry:sentry-instrument: Instrument an application with Sentry — detect the platform, install and initialize the SDK if needed, and wire up any signal — error monitoring, tracing/performance, logging, metrics, profiling, session replay, user feedback, cron check-ins, and AI/LLM monitoring. Use to add Sentry to a project or to capture more than errors.\n- sentry:sentry-workflow: Review code and keep Sentry SDKs up to date. Use when asked to resolve Sentry bot comments on a PR, address Seer bug predictions in PR reviews, or upgrade the Sentry SDK across major versions.\n- chrome-devtools-mcp:a11y-debugging: Uses Chrome DevTools MCP for accessibility (a11y) debugging and auditing based on web.dev guidelines. Use when testing semantic HTML, ARIA labels, focus states, keyboard navigation, tap targets, and color contrast.\n- chrome-devtools-mcp:chrome-devtools: Uses Chrome DevTools via MCP for efficient debugging, troubleshooting and browser automation. Use when debugging web pages, automating browser interactions, analyzing performance, or inspecting network requests. This skill does not apply to `--slim` mode (MCP configuration).\n- chrome-devtools-mcp:chrome-devtools-cli: Use this skill to write shell scripts or run shell commands to automate tasks in the browser or otherwise use Chrome DevTools via CLI.\n- chrome-devtools-mcp:debug-optimize-lcp: Guides debugging and optimizing Largest Contentful Paint (LCP) using Chrome DevTools MCP tools. Use this skill whenever the user asks about LCP performance, slow page loads, Core Web Vitals optimization, or wants to understand why their page's main content takes too long to appear. Also use when the user mentions \"largest contentful paint\", \"page load speed\", \"CWV\", or wants to improve how fast their hero image or main content renders.\n- chrome-devtools-mcp:memory-leak-debugging: Diagnoses and resolves memory leaks in JavaScript/Node.js applications. Use when a user reports high memory usage, OOM errors, or wants to capture, compare, or inspect heap snapshots with Chrome DevTools MCP memory tools.\n- chrome-devtools-mcp:troubleshooting: Uses Chrome DevTools MCP and documentation to troubleshoot connection and target issues. Trigger this skill when list_pages, new_page, or navigate_page fail, or when the server initialization fails.\n- expo:eas-app-stores: EAS service (paid). Deploy Expo apps to the app stores with EAS - build and submit to the iOS App Store, Google Play Store, and TestFlight, configure eas.json build and submit profiles, manage app versions and build numbers, and publish App Store metadata and ASO. Use whenever the user wants to deploy, release, or ship an app to production or the app stores, is preparing a production build, running eas build or eas submit, shipping to TestFlight, bumping version or build numbers, or setting up store listing metadata. For deploying an Expo website or API routes, use the eas-hosting skill.\n- expo:eas-hosting: EAS service (paid). Deploy Expo websites and Expo Router API routes to EAS Hosting - export the web bundle, run eas deploy for production and PR preview URLs, manage environment secrets and custom domains, and work within the Cloudflare Workers runtime. Also covers authoring API routes (+api.ts handlers, HTTP methods, request handling, CORS). Use when deploying an Expo web app or API routes, setting up EAS Hosting, or configuring hosting environments and domains. Not for native builds or store releases - use the eas-app-stores skill for those.\n- expo:eas-observe: EAS service (paid). Use for anything related to EAS Observe - adding `expo-observe` to an Expo project (AppMetricsRoot/ObserveRoot HOC, markInteractive, the useObserve hook, the Expo Router / React Navigation integrations for per-route metrics, and user-defined events via `Observe.logEvent`), querying via the EAS CLI (`eas observe:metrics-summary`, `observe:metrics`, `observe:routes`, `observe:events`, `observe:versions`), or interpreting the resulting metrics (cold/warm launch, TTR, TTI, navigation cold/warm TTR, update download, and the TTI frameRate params for triaging slow startups).\n- expo:eas-simulator: EAS service (paid). Run and control a user's app on a remote iOS/Android simulator hosted on EAS cloud. Read before running any `eas simulator:*` commands - it has the current syntax for this experimental API. Use whenever the user needs a simulator they can't run locally - 'run my app on a cloud simulator', 'use eas simulator to run/install/screenshot my app', 'I'm on Linux/Cursor and need an iOS device', 'no sim on this box / headless CI', 'let an agent click through my app and screenshot it', 'test my dev build on a remote sim with live reload', 'stream a sim to my browser' - even when they don't say 'EAS Simulator' or 'cloud'. On a host WITHOUT a local simulator (Linux, CI, cloud sandbox) it's the default; on macOS, do NOT auto-trigger for a plain 'run on the simulator' - use it only for a cloud/remote/shareable sim, an iOS version they lack, or an agent-driven session. NOT for local sims (expo run:ios, Xcode, Android Studio), EAS Build/Update, web preview, or physical devices.\n- expo:eas-update-insights: EAS service (paid). Check the health of published EAS Update: crash rates, install/launch counts, unique users, payload size, and the split between embedded and OTA users per channel. Use when the user asks how an update is performing, whether a rollout is healthy, how many users are on the embedded build vs OTA, or wants to gate CI on update health.\n- expo:eas-workflows: EAS service (paid). Helps understand and write EAS workflow YAML files for Expo projects. Use this skill when the user asks about CI/CD or workflows in an Expo or EAS context, mentions .eas/workflows/, or wants help with EAS build pipelines or deployment automation.\n- expo:expo-app-clip: Framework (OSS). Add an iOS App Clip target to an Expo app. Use when the user mentions App Clip, AASA, apple-app-site-association, appclips, smart app banner, or wants to ship a lightweight iOS Clip invoked from a URL alongside their parent app.\n- expo:expo-brownfield: Framework (OSS). Integrate Expo and React Native into an existing native iOS or Android app. Use when the user mentions brownfield, embedding React Native in a native app, AAR/XCFramework, or adding Expo to an existing Kotlin/Swift project. Covers both the isolated approach and the integrated approach.\n- expo:expo-data-fetching: Framework (OSS). Use when implementing or debugging ANY network request, API call, or data fetching. Covers fetch API, React Query, SWR, error handling, caching, offline support, and Expo Router data loaders (`useLoaderData`).\n- expo:expo-dev-client: Framework (OSS). Build and distribute Expo development clients locally or via TestFlight for internal testing. For production TestFlight releases and store submission, use the eas-app-stores skill.\n- expo:expo-dom: Framework (OSS). Use Expo DOM components to run web code in a webview on native and as-is on web. Migrate web code to native incrementally. For the end-to-end migration of a whole web app, use the expo-web-to-native skill.\n- expo:expo-examples: Framework (OSS). Expo's official example projects - the expo/examples repo of ~70 `with-*` integrations (Stripe, Clerk, Supabase, OpenAI, maps, Reanimated, SQLite, Skia, NativeWind, and more). Use when integrating a third-party library or service into an existing Expo app and you want the canonical, version-matched pattern to adapt, or when scaffolding a new project from one with `npx create-expo --example`.\n- expo:expo-module: Framework (OSS). Guide for creating and writing Expo native modules and views using the Expo Modules API (Swift, Kotlin, TypeScript). Covers module definition DSL, native views, shared objects, config plugins, lifecycle hooks, autolinking, and type system. Use when building or modifying native modules for Expo. Not for migrating an existing Swift module from the definition DSL to the Expo Modules API 2.0 macros; use expo-migrate-module (from the expo-experiments plugin) for that.\n- expo:expo-native-ui: Framework (OSS). Build beautiful, native-feeling Expo screens. Covers Apple HIG styling, semantic colors, native controls, SF Symbols, media, animations, visual effects, gradients, storage, and responsive layout. For routing and navigation, use the expo-router skill.\n- expo:expo-project-structure: Framework (OSS). Folder structure for a new Expo app. Use when scaffolding or laying out a new Expo project with Expo Router, or deciding where a file should live in one. For new projects only — never restructure an existing app to match.\n- expo:expo-router: Framework (OSS). Navigation and routing for Expo Router. Covers file-based routes, groups and dynamic routes, folder organization, Link with previews and context menus, native Stack, page titles, modals and form sheets, NativeTabs, headers and toolbars, and header search bars.\n- expo:expo-skill-feedback: Submit feedback on an Expo skill—or Expo itself—and control bundled anonymous usage telemetry (off by default / opt-in). Submit feedback with: npx --yes submit-expo-feedback@latest \"ACTIONABLE_FEEDBACK\". Optionally add either or both: --category \"CATEGORY\" and --subject \"SUBJECT\". Replace the uppercase placeholders before running. Use when a skill was useful, confusing, broken, missing context, or worth improving; when Expo, Expo CLI, EAS CLI, docs, or MCP worked well or fell short; or when the user explicitly asks to enable or disable telemetry, check its status, or understand what it collects.\n- expo:expo-tailwind-setup: Framework (OSS). Set up Tailwind CSS v4 in Expo with react-native-css and NativeWind v5 for universal styling\n- expo:expo-ui: Framework (OSS). Build native UI with the @expo/ui package: real SwiftUI on iOS and Jetpack Compose on Android rendered from React in an Expo or React Native app. Covers universal cross-platform components (Host, Column, Row, Button, Text, List, and more imported from @expo/ui), drop-in replacements for popular React Native community libraries (BottomSheet, DateTimePicker, Slider, Menu, etc.), and platform-specific SwiftUI (@expo/ui/swift-ui, iOS only) and Jetpack Compose (@expo/ui/jetpack-compose, Android only) trees and modifiers. Use when adding or reviewing @expo/ui Host/RNHostView trees, building native-feeling UI where standard React Native components fall short (grouped settings forms with toggles, sections, menus, sheets, pickers, sliders), choosing between universal and platform-specific components, or replacing an RN community UI library with a native @expo/ui equivalent. Not for custom native modules, Expo Router navigation, Reanimated, or data fetching.\n- expo:expo-upgrade: Framework (OSS). Guidelines for upgrading Expo SDK versions and fixing dependency issues\n- expo:expo-web-to-native: Framework (OSS). Migrate an existing web React app to a native iOS/Android app with Expo. Use when the user wants to turn a website into a mobile app, port a Next.js/Vite/CRA React codebase to React Native, reuse web code on native incrementally, or asks how web idioms (the DOM, CSS, React Router, localStorage, window) map to native. This is the end-to-end migration guide; use the `expo-dom` skill for the DOM-component mechanism itself.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- claude-in-chrome: Automates your Chrome browser to interact with web pages - clicking elements, filling forms, capturing screenshots, reading console logs, and navigating sites. Opens pages in new tabs within your existing Chrome session. Requires site-level permissions before executing (configured in the extension). - When the user wants to interact with web pages, automate browser tasks, capture screenshots, read console logs, or perform any browser-based actions. Always invoke BEFORE attempting to use any mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools.\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; for your working diff use /code-review\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch","skillCount":47,"isInitial":true,"names":["sentry:sentry-debug-issue","sentry:sentry-feature-setup","sentry:sentry-get-started","sentry:sentry-instrument","sentry:sentry-workflow","chrome-devtools-mcp:a11y-debugging","chrome-devtools-mcp:chrome-devtools","chrome-devtools-mcp:chrome-devtools-cli","chrome-devtools-mcp:debug-optimize-lcp","chrome-devtools-mcp:memory-leak-debugging","chrome-devtools-mcp:troubleshooting","expo:eas-app-stores","expo:eas-hosting","expo:eas-observe","expo:eas-simulator","expo:eas-update-insights","expo:eas-workflows","expo:expo-app-clip","expo:expo-brownfield","expo:expo-data-fetching","expo:expo-dev-client","expo:expo-dom","expo:expo-examples","expo:expo-module","expo:expo-native-ui","expo:expo-project-structure","expo:expo-router","expo:expo-skill-feedback","expo:expo-tailwind-setup","expo:expo-ui","expo:expo-upgrade","expo:expo-web-to-native","dataviz","artifact-design","artifact-capabilities","update-config","keybindings-help","simplify","fewer-permission-prompts","loop","schedule","claude-api","claude-in-chrome","run","init","review","security-review"]},"type":"attachment","uuid":"e9ad489e-5b7b-4cfa-8bab-ee4bfec1c8db","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:37:56.918Z","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"b014785b-4a32-45d2-ad72-eacbf9bd6afe","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01B1CcWvAh8U4BZ8UqL8ckq7","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"nut.js nut-tree licensing 2025 discontinued proprietary\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"nut-tree repositories · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/orgs/nut-tree/repositories\"},{\"title\":\"nut-tree · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/nut-tree\"},{\"title\":\"End User License Agreement | nut.js | nut.js\",\"url\":\"https://nutjs.dev/legal/eula\"},{\"title\":\"I'm giving up — on open source | nut.js\",\"url\":\"https://nutjs.dev/blog/i-give-up\"},{\"title\":\"nut.js/README.md at develop · nut-tree/nut.js · ...\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/nut-tree/nut.js/blob/develop/README.md\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - nut-tree/nut.js: Native UI testing / controlling with node · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/nut-tree/nut.js/\"},{\"title\":\"nut.js - Desktop Automation for Node.js\",\"url\":\"https://nutjs.dev/\"},{\"title\":\"nut-tree-fork/nut-js\",\"url\":\"https://classic.yarnpkg.com/en/package/@nut-tree-fork/nut-js\"},{\"title\":\"@nut-tree-fork/nut-js - npm\",\"url\":\"https://www.npmjs.com/package/@nut-tree-fork/nut-js\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found regarding nut.js licensing and its shift in 2024-2025:\n\n## Licensing Change from Open Source to Proprietary\n\nnut.js was originally started under the Apache-2.0 license, but the project has transitioned to a proprietary licensing model. The project now offers Package Licenses (for single products like @nut-tree/bolt, @nut-tree/nl-matcher, or @nut-tree/plugin-ocr), Solo Licenses (for one authorized user), and Team Licenses (for multiple authorized users within a single organization).\n\n## Open Source Status\n\nDespite the licensing change, nut.js remains open source and free to use if you build it from sources. Updates to the repository continue with a delay, with new features, patches, bug fixes and security updates being made available to subscribers first.\n\n## Not Discontinued\n\nThe project has not been discontinued. The nut.js repository shows activity as recently as May 1, 2024, and the project continues active development with a commercial licensing model for those who want pre-built packages and priority updates.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"81497dbc-626e-4c5f-82e1-0642bd999fd6","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:39:12.953Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"nut.js nut-tree licensing 2025 discontinued proprietary","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01Daa7Ny2qpXKPF9Lwuwe6jG","content":[{"title":"nut-tree repositories · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/orgs/nut-tree/repositories"},{"title":"nut-tree · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/nut-tree"},{"title":"End User License Agreement | nut.js | nut.js","url":"https://nutjs.dev/legal/eula"},{"title":"I'm giving up — on open source | nut.js","url":"https://nutjs.dev/blog/i-give-up"},{"title":"nut.js/README.md at develop · nut-tree/nut.js · ...","url":"https://github.com/nut-tree/nut.js/blob/develop/README.md"},{"title":"GitHub - nut-tree/nut.js: Native UI testing / controlling with node · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/nut-tree/nut.js/"},{"title":"nut.js - Desktop Automation for Node.js","url":"https://nutjs.dev/"},{"title":"nut-tree-fork/nut-js","url":"https://classic.yarnpkg.com/en/package/@nut-tree-fork/nut-js"},{"title":"@nut-tree-fork/nut-js - npm","url":"https://www.npmjs.com/package/@nut-tree-fork/nut-js"}]},"Based on the search results, here's what I found regarding nut.js licensing and its shift in 2024-2025:\n\n## Licensing Change from Open Source to Proprietary\n\nnut.js was originally started under the Apache-2.0 license, but the project has transitioned to a proprietary licensing model. The project now offers Package Licenses (for single products like @nut-tree/bolt, @nut-tree/nl-matcher, or @nut-tree/plugin-ocr), Solo Licenses (for one authorized user), and Team Licenses (for multiple authorized users within a single organization).\n\n## Open Source Status\n\nDespite the licensing change, nut.js remains open source and free to use if you build it from sources. hurdlegroup/robotjs: Node.js Desktop Automation.\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/hurdlegroup/robotjs\"},{\"title\":\"Top 2 Alternatives to RobotJS for Desktop automation - TestDriver\",\"url\":\"https://testdriver.ai/articles/top-2-alternatives-to-robotjs-for-desktop-automation\"},{\"title\":\"Top 23 Open Source Alternatives to RobotJS - TestDriver\",\"url\":\"https://testdriver.ai/articles/top-23-open-source-alternatives-to-robotjs\"},{\"title\":\"Swap out Node.js module for simulating keypresses in Windows · Issue #25 · w3c/aria-at-automation-driver\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/w3c/aria-at-automation-driver/issues/25\"},{\"title\":\"Top 23 Alternatives to RobotJS for Node.js Testing - TestDriver\",\"url\":\"https://testdriver.ai/articles/top-23-alternatives-to-robotjs-for-node-js-testing\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - jitsi/robotjs: Node.js Desktop Automation. · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/jitsi/robotjs\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about RobotJS and its alternatives for Node.js desktop automation:\n\n## RobotJS Maintenance Status\n\nThe original RobotJS by OctalMage is unmaintained, and the package cannot be installed using current versions of npm.\n\n## Active Forks and Maintained Alternatives\n\nThere are several maintained forks of RobotJS available:\n\n1. **@hurdlegroup/robotjs** - This is a fork of the original unmaintained RobotJS that supports Mac, Windows, and Linux.\n\n2. **@jitsi/robotjs** - This repository is a fork of the original robotjs to generate prebuilt packages, with all changes upstreamed.\n\n## Other Desktop Automation Alternatives\n\nFor Node.js developers seeking alternatives, Nut.js is mentioned as a more widely-used module for desktop automation.\n\n## Use Cases and Limitations\n\nRobotJS remains a useful tool when you need simple, OS-level automation from Node.js with cross-platform support and direct control over keyboard and mouse. However, Node native modules sometimes require compilers or platform-specific dependencies to build correctly, which can complicate CI and containerized environments.\n\nIf you need more advanced capabilities, tools like FlaUI and WinAppDriver provide richer UI automation for Windows, while SikuliX's image-based approach can help for cross-platform desktop UIs.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"3ede8f27-7954-41fb-ab55-006b28423a92","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:39:15.750Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"robotjs unmaintained 2025 alternative node desktop automation","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01FSBpvUg5cYukV9y2ZiQ1XU","content":[{"title":"GitHub - TestDriver","url":"https://testdriver.ai/articles/top-23-open-source-alternatives-to-robotjs"},{"title":"Swap out Node.js module for simulating keypresses in Windows · Issue #25 · w3c/aria-at-automation-driver","url":"https://github.com/w3c/aria-at-automation-driver/issues/25"},{"title":"Top 23 Alternatives to RobotJS for Node.js Testing - TestDriver","url":"https://testdriver.ai/articles/top-23-alternatives-to-robotjs-for-node-js-testing"},{"title":"GitHub - jitsi/robotjs: Node.js Desktop Automation. · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/jitsi/robotjs"}]},"Based on the search results, here's what I found about RobotJS and its alternatives for Node.js desktop automation:\n\n## RobotJS Maintenance Status\n\nThe original RobotJS by OctalMage is unmaintained, and the package cannot be installed using current versions of npm.\n\n## Active Forks and Maintained Alternatives\n\nThere are several maintained forks of RobotJS available:\n\n1. **@hurdlegroup/robotjs** - This is a fork of the original unmaintained RobotJS that supports Mac, Windows, and Linux.\n\n2. **@jitsi/robotjs** - This repository is a fork of the original robotjs to generate prebuilt packages, with all changes upstreamed.\n\n## Other Desktop Automation Alternatives\n\nFor Node.js developers seeking alternatives, Nut.js is mentioned as a more widely-used module for desktop automation.\n\n## Use Cases and Limitations\n\nRobotJS remains a useful tool when you need simple, OS-level automation from Node.js with cross-platform support and direct control over keyboard and mouse. However, Node native modules sometimes require compilers or platform-specific dependencies to build correctly, which can complicate CI and containerized environments.\n\nIf you need more advanced capabilities, tools like FlaUI and WinAppDriver provide richer UI automation for Windows, while SikuliX's image-based approach can help for cross-platform desktop UIs."],"durationSeconds":7.564634250000119,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"2c8e8b33-365f-4a8c-9917-6bc7e6d32d77","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"716c7f84-6e69-40bb-a793-ad2231e05c0a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Jm5UHbLpuyqJngr66abzoc","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"ydotool vs dotool vs wtype Wayland input automation 2025\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"GitHub - ReimuNotMoe/ydotool: Generic command-line automation tool · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/ReimuNotMoe/ydotool\"},{\"title\":\"Wlrctl: the best Wayland replacement to xdotool - Raspberry Pi Forums\",\"url\":\"https://forums.raspberrypi.com/viewtopic.php?t=371406\"},{\"title\":\"Sending Keyboard Strokes to Wayland Linux Windows: Solutions and Challenges\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/@python-javascript-php-html-css/sending-keyboard-strokes-to-wayland-linux-windows-solutions-and-challenges-9319cf424d06\"},{\"title\":\"ydotool is an alternative to xdotool that works on both X11 and Wayland\",\"url\":\"https://gadgeteer.co.za/ydotool-is-an-alternative-to-xdotool-that-works-on-both-x11-and-wayland/\"},{\"title\":\"Use dotool or ydotool to support Wayland? · Issue #656 · JoseExposito/touchegg\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/JoseExposito/touchegg/issues/656\"},{\"title\":\"Software Alternatives, Accelerators & Startups\",\"url\":\"https://www.saashub.com/compare-keystarter-vs-xdotool\"},{\"title\":\"wtype Alternatives\",\"url\":\"https://alternativeto.net/software/wtype/\"},{\"title\":\"ydotool Alternatives\",\"url\":\"https://alternativeto.net/software/ydotool\"},{\"title\":\"Software Alternatives, Accelerators & Startups\",\"url\":\"https://www.saashub.com/compare-xdotool-vs-repeat\"},{\"title\":\"Software Alternatives, Accelerators & Startups\",\"url\":\"https://www.saashub.com/compare-xdotool-vs-zennoposter\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here's a comparison of these three Wayland input automation tools:\n\n## ydotool\n\nydotool uses the uinput framework of Linux kernel to emulate an input device, which allows it to work across multiple platforms. ydotool is not limited to Wayland—you can use it on anything as long as it accepts keyboard/mouse input.\n\nHowever, there are some limitations: ydotool does keystrokes and mouse actions quite well but it does not do window functions like resize, move, bring into focus, or direct actions to background windows. Additionally, ydotool failed to handle non-ASCII characters or run consistently in some environments.\n\n## wtype\n\nwtype is described as 'Xdotool type for wayland'. Wtype does not require sudo and does work inside nested wayland compositors, but it only allows for simulating keyboard events with no support for selecting a window to send events to.\n\n## dotool\n\nWhile the search results mention dotool as an xdotool alternative, specific technical details about dotool are limited. dotool can handle Wayland window commands like closing windows with key commands, and non-working presets can be replaced with equivalent dotool commands to work in Wayland.\n\n## Summary\n\nEach tool has trade-offs: ydotool offers broader functionality but may have consistency issues, wtype is lightweight for keyboard input but lacks mouse control and window targeting, and dotool appears to be another viable option for Wayland automation.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"53e45871-07ca-4358-b263-1d4bc8d878bc","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:39:16.309Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"ydotool vs dotool vs wtype Wayland input automation 2025","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01HSu8AUvsxP5VMcxEnvEW2L","content":[{"title":"GitHub - ReimuNotMoe/ydotool: Generic command-line automation tool · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/ReimuNotMoe/ydotool"},{"title":"Wlrctl: the best Wayland replacement to xdotool - Raspberry Pi Forums","url":"https://forums.raspberrypi.com/viewtopic.php?t=371406"},{"title":"Sending Keyboard Strokes to Wayland Linux Windows: Solutions and Challenges","url":"https://medium.com/@python-javascript-php-html-css/sending-keyboard-strokes-to-wayland-linux-windows-solutions-and-challenges-9319cf424d06"},{"title":"ydotool is an alternative to xdotool that works on both X11 and Wayland","url":"https://gadgeteer.co.za/ydotool-is-an-alternative-to-xdotool-that-works-on-both-x11-and-wayland/"},{"title":"Use dotool or ydotool to support Wayland? · Issue #656 · JoseExposito/touchegg","url":"https://github.com/JoseExposito/touchegg/issues/656"},{"title":"Software Alternatives, Accelerators & Startups","url":"https://www.saashub.com/compare-keystarter-vs-xdotool"},{"title":"wtype Alternatives","url":"https://alternativeto.net/software/wtype/"},{"title":"ydotool Alternatives","url":"https://alternativeto.net/software/ydotool"},{"title":"Software Alternatives, Accelerators & Startups","url":"https://www.saashub.com/compare-xdotool-vs-repeat"},{"title":"Software Alternatives, Accelerators & Startups","url":"https://www.saashub.com/compare-xdotool-vs-zennoposter"}]},"Based on the web search results, here's a comparison of these three Wayland input automation tools:\n\n## ydotool\n\nydotool uses the uinput framework of Linux kernel to emulate an input device, which allows it to work across multiple platforms. ydotool is not limited to Wayland—you can use it on anything as long as it accepts keyboard/mouse input.\n\nHowever, there are some limitations: ydotool does keystrokes and mouse actions quite well but it does not do window functions like resize, move, bring into focus, or direct actions to background windows. Additionally, ydotool failed to handle non-ASCII characters or run consistently in some environments.\n\n## wtype\n\nwtype is described as 'Xdotool type for wayland'. Wtype does not require sudo and does work inside nested wayland compositors, but it only allows for simulating keyboard events with no support for selecting a window to send events to.\n\n## dotool\n\nWhile the search results mention dotool as an xdotool alternative, specific technical details about dotool are limited. dotool can handle Wayland window commands like closing windows with key commands, and non-working presets can be replaced with equivalent dotool commands to work in Wayland.\n\n## Summary\n\nEach tool has trade-offs: ydotool offers broader functionality but may have consistency issues, wtype is lightweight for keyboard input but lacks mouse control and window targeting, and dotool appears to be another viable option for Wayland automation."],"durationSeconds":8.217098750000819,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"716c7f84-6e69-40bb-a793-ad2231e05c0a","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}

If you can see it, you can automate it.\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/oculix-org/Oculix\"},{\"title\":\"SikuliX download | SourceForge.net\",\"url\":\"https://sourceforge.net/projects/sikulix.mirror/\"},{\"title\":\"Top 15 Alternatives to SikuliX for Linux, Windows, macOS Testing - TestDriver\",\"url\":\"https://testdriver.ai/articles/top-15-alternatives-to-sikulix-for-linux-windows-macos-testing\"},{\"title\":\"SikuliX was a UI automation tool from over a decade ago with an IDE that looked ... | Hacker News\",\"url\":\"https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48672856\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about SikuliX's status and its relationship to OculiX:\n\n## SikuliX Development Status\n\nRaiMan has finally stopped development in 2025, ending over a decade of stewardship of the project. RaiMan maintained SikuliX from 2010–2023 through 13 years of development, evolving it significantly during that time.\n\n## OculiX as the Successor\n\nJulien Mer has taken over the further development as OculiX. More specifically, in March 2026, RaiMan archived the upstream and transmitted stewardship; active development continues as OculiX.\n\nOculiX is the evolution of SikuliX, actively maintained with the full agreement of its original creator RaiMan, and it is the active continuation of the Sikuli (Tom Yeh, MIT UIST 2009) and SikuliX (Raimund Hocke, 2010–2025) lineage.\n\n## Current OculiX Development\n\nSikuliX is actively maintained under oculix-org with the current stable version being 2.0.5. OculiX's current releases include v3.0.3 stable and v4.0 in flight with Java 17+ support. The modern continuation features OpenCV 4.10, Java 17+, working VNC stack, Android 12+ ADB, and PaddleOCR option.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"304af124-f7dd-4d0e-b2f3-e91945c9dbbe","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:39:19.718Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"SikuliX status 2025 OculiX successor","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01NZgz7MGuHwNdKvuvDR8nry","content":[{"title":"SikuliX and Oculix Documentation | SikuliX / Oculix","url":"https://docs.oculix.org/"},{"title":"RaiMan's SikuliX is Julien Mer’s Oculix | sikulix.com","url":"https://raimans-sikulix.gitbook.io/sikulix.com"},{"title":"SikuliX and Oculix Documentation — SikuliX 2.x+ documentation","url":"https://sikulix-2014.readthedocs.io/"},{"title":"AskUI vs SikuliX: Agentic Testing vs Image-Based Automation (2026) | AskUI Blog","url":"https://www.askui.com/blog-posts/askui-vs-sikulix-visual-automation-comparison-2025"},{"title":"OculiX download | SourceForge.net","url":"https://sourceforge.net/projects/oculix/"},{"title":"GitHub - oculix-org/SikuliX1: SikuliX version 2.0.0+ (2019+) · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/oculix-org/SikuliX1"},{"title":"GitHub - oculix-org/Oculix: OculiX — Visual Automation IDE. RaiMan maintained SikuliX from 2010–2023 through 13 years of development, evolving it significantly during that time.\n\n## OculiX as the Successor\n\nJulien Mer has taken over the further development as OculiX. More specifically, in March 2026, RaiMan archived the upstream and transmitted stewardship; active development continues as OculiX.\n\nOculiX is the evolution of SikuliX, actively maintained with the full agreement of its original creator RaiMan, and it is the active continuation of the Sikuli (Tom Yeh, MIT UIST 2009) and SikuliX (Raimund Hocke, 2010–2025) lineage.\n\n## Current OculiX Development\n\nSikuliX is actively maintained under oculix-org with the current stable version being 2.0.5. OculiX's current releases include v3.0.3 stable and v4.0 in flight with Java 17+ support. The modern continuation features OpenCV 4.10, Java 17+, working VNC stack, Android 12+ ADB, and PaddleOCR option."],"durationSeconds":8.119732791999356,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"cfef0f06-4ee4-4e4d-925a-a1e149cacaa3","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"f467a2c4-6144-4118-9761-f078bd0f539a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HM9vJjeK36ZfF1UtKpXejp","type":"tool_result","content":"# nut.js Open Source Status Change Summary\n\n**What Happened:** On May 2, 2024, nut.js transitioned from an open-source Apache-2.0 licensed project to a subscription-based commercial model.\n\n**Why:** The maintainer, Simon Hofmann, cited frustration with the sustainability of open source work. He noted that despite making the software permissively available, companies and individuals rarely provided financial support. Instead, he received complaints and eventually public accusations of \"selling out.\" He stated: \"if people start insulting you for something you're doing in your free time, it's time to stop.\"\n\n**New Licensing Model:** Ready-to-use packages are now exclusively available through a private nut.js package registry requiring an active subscription. Prices will increase with new plugin releases, though existing subscribers are grandfathered in.\n\n**GitHub Repository:** The GitHub repo remains publicly accessible, allowing developers to build, test, and host packages independently if they choose not to subscribe.\n\n**Timeline:** The shift toward commercial plugins began approximately 2.5 years before this announcement (around late 2021), though the complete transition occurred in May 2024."}]},"uuid":"75749862-359d-4a39-9094-be14cabaff82","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:39:34.748Z","toolUseResult":{"bytes":63531,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# nut.js Open Source Status Change Summary\n\n**What Happened:** On May 2, 2024, nut.js transitioned from an open-source Apache-2.0 licensed project to a subscription-based commercial model.\n\n**Why:** The maintainer, Simon Hofmann, cited frustration with the sustainability of open source work. He noted that despite making the software permissively available, companies and individuals rarely provided financial support. Instead, he received complaints and eventually public accusations of \"selling out.\" He stated: \"if people start insulting you for something you're doing in your free time, it's time to stop.\"\n\n**New Licensing Model:** Ready-to-use packages are now exclusively available through a private nut.js package registry requiring an active subscription. Prices will increase with new plugin releases, though existing subscribers are grandfathered in.\n\n**GitHub Repository:** The GitHub repo remains publicly accessible, allowing developers to build, test, and host packages independently if they choose not to subscribe.\n\n**Timeline:** The shift toward commercial plugins began approximately 2.5 years before this announcement (around late 2021), though the complete transition occurred in May 2024.","durationMs":4204,"url":"https://nutjs.dev/blog/i-give-up"},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f467a2c4-6144-4118-9761-f078bd0f539a","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}

However, there is an effort to address this: A .NET rewrite called KeySharp is in alpha and aims to be cross-platform, but it's not production-ready.\n\n## Known Limitations & Complaints\n\nThe search results indicate that AutoHotkey v2 has specific limitations reflected in discussions: Not great for heavy computing, concurrency, or cross-platform packaging. Some users note that AutoHotkey's scripting language uses a non-standard syntax—it's neither BASIC nor Python nor JavaScript, so developers coming from other languages need to learn AutoHotkey's specific conventions for objects, functions, and control flow.\n\nThe main complaint relevant to your query is the Windows-only constraint, though the community is working on cross-platform alternatives like KeySharp that may eventually address this limitation.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"0b7518c7-3d87-4574-aa33-52bcf968d3ad","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:39:35.203Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"AutoHotkey v2 2025 state cross-platform Windows-only complaints","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01Ajf9U75iaFsk7j63tQteeX","content":[{"title":"The Best AutoHotkey Alternatives in 2026","url":"https://textexpander.com/blog/autohotkey-alternatives"},{"title":"AutoHotkey v2 – the-Automator","url":"https://www.the-automator.com/tag/autohotkey-v2/"},{"title":"AutoIt vs AutoHotkey: Which Windows Automation Tool Is Right for You?","url":"https://textexpander.com/blog/autoit-vs-autohotkey"},{"title":"AutoHotkey cross-platform comparison","url":"https://www.the-automator.com/tag/autohotkey-cross-platform-comparison/"},{"title":"Changes & New Features | AutoHotkey v2","url":"https://www.autohotkey.com/docs/v2/ChangeLog.htm"},{"title":"Windows Server 2025","url":"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windows_Server_2025"},{"title":"AutoHotkey","url":"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AutoHotkey"},{"title":"AutoIt","url":"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AutoIt"}]},"Based on the search results, here's what I found regarding AutoHotkey v2 and cross-platform/Windows-only issues:\n\n## Current State of AutoHotkey v2\n\nAutoHotkey v2.0 was released in December 2022 and continues to receive updates. {"parentUuid":"d098deaf-0a03-4287-a2de-2e72895b7a56","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018MESf4NEBHeVx3mT2E7bgq","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"pyautogui Wayland not working 2025 alternative Linux\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"PyAutoGUI issues: Only works for x11 on Linux systems · Issue #6 · DonTizi/ReMind\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/DonTizi/ReMind/issues/6\"},{\"title\":\"PyAutoGUI usage with default Gnome Wayland (Not the Gnome with Xorg) · Issue #740 · asweigart/pyautogui\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/asweigart/pyautogui/issues/740\"},{\"title\":\"Wayland Support for `PyAutoGUI` actions · Issue #4010 · seleniumbase/SeleniumBase\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/seleniumbase/SeleniumBase/issues/4010\"},{\"title\":\"[Question] Does pyautogui work with wayland? · Issue #747 · asweigart/pyautogui\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/asweigart/pyautogui/issues/747\"},{\"title\":\"Support for Wayland · Issue #111 · asweigart/pyautogui\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/asweigart/pyautogui/issues/111\"},{\"title\":\"github.com\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/pygame-community/pygame-ce/issues/2799\"},{\"title\":\"Wayland AlternativesWindow Managers and other similar apps like Wayland\",\"url\":\"https://alternativeto.net/software/wayland\"},{\"title\":\"github.com\",\"url\":\"https://github.com:443/jordansissel/xdotool\"},{\"title\":\"Wayland Software Page 2 of 4\",\"url\":\"https://alternativeto.net/platform/wayland/?p=2\"},{\"title\":\"pypi.org\",\"url\":\"https://pypi.org/project/PyAutoGUI/0.9.1\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about PyAutoGUI and Wayland compatibility:\n\n## The Problem\n\nPyAutoGUI only supports X11, and using PyAutoGUI with the Wayland windowing system results in image not found errors and screenshots that are completely black. Wayland is a very different graphics system than X11, and while Wayland has some X11 compatibility, many things do not work correctly with tools like xdotool, including typing, window searching, and many other functions.\n\n## Recommended Alternatives\n\nSuggested fallback mechanisms for Wayland environments include python-evdev, pynput, or D-Bus. Additionally, for users on Wayland, alternative tools include ydotool and dotool, which send mouse and keyboard events using Linux's uinput system.\n\n## Workaround\n\nA common workaround mentioned in the search results is to use GNOME with Xorg instead of Wayland, though this may not be ideal depending on your use case.\n\nThe search results show this has been a long-standing issue (dating back to at least 2016), and the PyAutoGUI repository has not been actively maintained.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"0bbaa72c-4180-4ab0-846f-4943eab140f2","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:39:35.837Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"pyautogui Wayland not working 2025 alternative Linux","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01TzGbhQfiK6TPjduN2hx2h3","content":[{"title":"PyAutoGUI issues: Only works for x11 on Linux systems · Issue #6 · DonTizi/ReMind","url":"https://github.com/DonTizi/ReMind/issues/6"},{"title":"PyAutoGUI usage with default Gnome Wayland (Not the Gnome with Xorg) · Issue #740 · asweigart/pyautogui","url":"https://github.com/asweigart/pyautogui/issues/740"},{"title":"Wayland Support for `PyAutoGUI` actions · Issue #4010 · seleniumbase/SeleniumBase","url":"https://github.com/seleniumbase/SeleniumBase/issues/4010"},{"title":"[Question] Does pyautogui work with wayland? Wayland is a very different graphics system than X11, and while Wayland has some X11 compatibility, many things do not work correctly with tools like xdotool, including typing, window searching, and many other functions.\n\n## Recommended Alternatives\n\nSuggested fallback mechanisms for Wayland environments include python-evdev, pynput, or D-Bus. {"parentUuid":"b7022f30-abb7-4c8e-8b78-de1268fb44e6","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_013Vmf76HuWCGzQg8WsWDWot","type":"tool_result","content":"# Hacker News Thread Summary: SikuliX UI Automation\n\n**Date:** 33 days ago\n\n**Submission Context:** Discussion thread titled \"Mixing Visual and Textual Code\"\n\n**Main Comment by Izkata:**\nIzkata described SikuliX as \"a UI automation tool from over a decade ago\" that enabled developers to blend visual and code-based approaches. The tool used Python as its underlying language but allowed IDE users to \"swap out certain functions' arguments with the image and targets\" rather than editing raw code directly. Notably, it featured built-in image manipulation capabilities accessible through the IDE interface.\n\n**Key Features Mentioned:**\n- Visual representation of automation targets (depicted as \"little red crosses\" in screenshots)\n- Direct in-IDE image and target editing without manual code modification\n- Python-based scripting foundation\n\n**Successor Tool:**\nIzkata briefly noted that \"it has a successor called Oculix\" but acknowledged having no detailed knowledge about it.\n\n**Follow-up Commentary:**\nadamhepner contributed that their recent SikuliX experience involved its Robot Framework library integration, describing it as \"a very neat way of both keeping the tech somewhat alive\" while improving script accessibility.\n\n**Notable Quote:**\n\"The language was just python and the IDE swapped out certain functions' arguments with the image\" (Izkata)"}]},"uuid":"e42d427a-4349-4046-95a2-4481bcf11d0f","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:39:51.078Z","toolUseResult":{"bytes":5249,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# Hacker News Thread Summary: SikuliX UI Automation\n\n**Date:** 33 days ago\n\n**Submission Context:** Discussion thread titled \"Mixing Visual and Textual Code\"\n\n**Main Comment by Izkata:**\nIzkata described SikuliX as \"a UI automation tool from over a decade ago\" that enabled developers to blend visual and code-based approaches. The tool used Python as its underlying language but allowed IDE users to \"swap out certain functions' arguments with the image and targets\" rather than editing raw code directly. Notably, it featured built-in image manipulation capabilities accessible through the IDE interface.\n\n**Key Features Mentioned:**\n- Visual representation of automation targets (depicted as \"little red crosses\" in screenshots)\n- Direct in-IDE image and target editing without manual code modification\n- Python-based scripting foundation\n\n**Successor Tool:**\nIzkata briefly noted that \"it has a successor called Oculix\" but acknowledged having no detailed knowledge about it.\n\n**Follow-up Commentary:**\nadamhepner contributed that their recent SikuliX experience involved its Robot Framework library integration, describing it as \"a very neat way of both keeping the tech somewhat alive\" while improving script accessibility.\n\n**Notable Quote:**\n\"The language was just python and the IDE swapped out certain functions' arguments with the image\" (Izkata)","durationMs":4732,"url":"https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48672856"},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b7022f30-abb7-4c8e-8b78-de1268fb44e6","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}

Here are the key findings:\n\n**Enigo - Cross-Platform Input Simulation Library:**\nEnigo is a cross-platform (Linux, Windows, macOS & BSD) library to simulate keyboard and mouse events. Claude Platform Docs\",\"url\":\"https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/computer-use-tool\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's how Anthropic's computer use reference implementation executes clicks using xdotool in Docker:\n\n## How Clicks Are Executed\n\nThe computer-use-demo example code defines a ComputerTool class which shells out to xdotool to move and click the mouse. More specifically, tool invocations are translated into xdotool invocations and bash commands.\n\n## The Process Flow\n\nWhen you ask Claude to click a button, it needs to translate \"the blue submit button in the lower right\" into exact pixel coordinates. Anthropic's solution was to train Claude to count pixels from reference points (screen edges, known UI elements) to target locations.\n\n## Docker Implementation\n\nThe Anthropic-quickstarts image provides a complete Ubuntu desktop with Xvfb virtual framebuffer, mutter window manager, tint2 taskbar, and Firefox pre-installed. Screenshots are captured via scrot; mouse and keyboard events are injected via xdotool.\n\n## Code Example\n\nFor clicks, xdotool is invoked with mousemove to move to coordinates, then click with button 1 is executed. This is done via subprocess calls that translate Claude's action requests into actual xdotool commands.\n\nThe entire process is containerized for safety and portability, with a Linux Docker container running a chatbot connected to Claude and executing the agent loop.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"9d74eaf3-63c2-4b27-ab8f-37dff33602d7","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:40:11.031Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"Anthropic computer use reference implementation xdotool docker how clicks executed","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_017QoLAjFEvn4ovJdgjMBTQc","content":[{"title":"LLMs can Control your Computer now - Anchor\",\"url\":\"https://anchorbrowser.io/blog/how-openai-operator-works-with-ai-agents\"},{\"title\":\"Computer use | OpenAI API\",\"url\":\"https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/tools-computer-use\"},{\"title\":\"OpenAI launches Operator, a new AI agent to browse and interact with websites via ChatGPT\",\"url\":\"https://alternativeto.net/news/2025/1/openai-launches-operator-a-new-ai-agent-to-browse-and-interact-with-websites-via-chatgpt\"}]\n\n## OpenAI Operator and ChatGPT Agent: How It Works\n\nBased on the search results, here's how OpenAI's Operator and ChatGPT agent execute clicks and interact with web pages:\n\n### Core Technology\n\nOpenAI's Operator runs on the Computer-Using Agent (CUA), which works through a virtual browser environment to process visual information and execute commands. OpenAI calls this their Computer Using Agent (CUA) model.\n\n### How It Executes Actions\n\nThe system captures screenshots of web interfaces and analyzes raw pixel data to find interactive elements like buttons and text fields. The model looks at the current UI through a screenshot, returns actions such as clicks, typing, or scrolling, and your harness executes those actions in a browser or computer environment.\n\n### Technical Process\n\nThe application fires up a miniaturized browser window which it controls through a combination of screenshots and text prompts. The model takes a screenshot of the browser window while navigating the internet, then uses a version of OpenAI's \"reasoning\" model to interpret and make decisions. The system uses chain-of-thought reasoning to break complex tasks into manageable steps. For example, it constantly scans the screen, takes action, reviews results, and adjusts its approach as needed.\n\n### Virtual Computer Environment\n\nChatGPT carries out these tasks using its own virtual computer, fluidly shifting between reasoning and action to handle complex workflows from start to finish, all based on your instructions.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"5269e2d9-2d3a-407e-9412-37d1dc578912","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:40:13.328Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"OpenAI Operator ChatGPT agent virtual computer how executes clicks CUA","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_0126ZmN4gKBgNp8XzndVyhgd","content":[{"title":"OpenAI's Operator Agent Clicks, Types, Browses Like Humans","url":"https://writesonic.com/blog/open-ai-launches-operator"},{"title":"Introducing Operator | OpenAI","url":"https://openai.com/index/introducing-operator/"},{"title":"Introducing ChatGPT agent: bridging research and action | OpenAI","url":"https://openai.com/index/introducing-chatgpt-agent/"},{"title":"ChatGPT agent | OpenAI Help Center","url":"https://help.openai.com/en/articles/11752874-chatgpt-agent"},{"title":"Hands on with OpenAI's Operator – Dr Leon Furze","url":"https://leonfurze.com/2025/02/28/hands-on-with-openais-operator/"},{"title":"OpenAI's Operator: Examples, Use Cases, Competition & More | DataCamp","url":"https://www.datacamp.com/blog/operator"},{"title":"OpenAI Operator Explained: How AI Agents Actually Control the Web -
