{"parentUuid":"4470479c-5c0c-4149-9065-e59fdafbf6ab","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0167ozvHwZkmmwRiFCqeotvA","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"accessibility tree automation limitations Electron canvas games Citrix \"computer vision\" UiPath why\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"UiPath Computer Vision (CV). Automation is evolving fast, and UiPath… | by Praveen BK | Dec, 2025 | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/@praveenb0927/uipath-computer-vision-cv-626387166168\"},{\"title\":\"Computer Vision in UiPath's Automation Ecosystem\",\"url\":\"https://www.ijirmps.org/papers/2025/4/232709.pdf\"},{\"title\":\"Citrix Automation | UiPath\",\"url\":\"https://www.uipath.com/platform/agentic-automation/ai-ecosystem/citrix-automation\"},{\"title\":\"Activities - Known issues and limitations\",\"url\":\"https://docs.uipath.com/activities/other/latest/ui-automation/known-issues-and-limitations-citrix\"},{\"title\":\"Robotics Process Automation and Citrix...A love Story\",\"url\":\"https://www.accelirate.com/challenges-of-rpa-in-citrix-environment/\"},{\"title\":\"Activities - v23.4\",\"url\":\"https://docs.uipath.com/activities/other/latest/ui-automation/release-notes-uipath-uiautomation-activities-v23-4\"},{\"title\":\"Activities - v24.10\",\"url\":\"https://docs.uipath.com/activities/other/latest/ui-automation/release-notes-uipath-uiautomation-activities-v24-10\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found regarding accessibility tree automation limitations and why computer vision is needed:\n\n## Accessibility Tree and Automation Limitations\n\nTraditional UiPath selectors rely on DOM, accessibility framework, or UI tree, but these approaches have significant constraints. In Citrix or any type of virtualized environment, the controls that RPA is able to use to manipulate the UI via Object IDs are not available.\n\n## Why Computer Vision is Necessary\n\nThe key limitation is that in virtualized environments, the bot is essentially looking at a \"picture,\" so every interaction the bot has will have to be based off of image recognition. UiPath Computer Vision is needed for automating remote desktops, virtual machines, Citrix apps, SAP screens, or image-based interfaces.\n\n## Specific Scenarios Where Computer Vision Applies\n\nDesktop apps that have no UI built with modern accessibility layers, Citrix or VDI without Remote Runtime as interface will be streamed as an image, and RDP sessions without extensions all require computer vision approaches.\n\n## Computer Vision Capabilities\n\nComputer vision breaks limitations by enabling robots to interact with any screen, regardless of technology, environment, or selector availability. UiPath has been ranked by Forrester/Gartner reports as the leader in Citrix/RDP automations, attributed mostly to their industry-leading computer vision capabilities.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"3f45e65b-680c-4035-972c-529f6bcb1560","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:40:33.537Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"accessibility tree automation limitations Electron canvas games Citrix \"computer vision\" UiPath why","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01UjPn4KxXijDcLS1JVTQWrP","content":[{"title":"UiPath Computer Vision (CV). Automation is evolving fast, and UiPath… | by Praveen BK | Dec, 2025 | Medium","url":"https://medium.com/@praveenb0927/uipath-computer-vision-cv-626387166168"},{"title":"Computer Vision in UiPath's Automation Ecosystem","url":"https://www.ijirmps.org/papers/2025/4/232709.pdf"},{"title":"Citrix Automation | UiPath","url":"https://www.uipath.com/platform/agentic-automation/ai-ecosystem/citrix-automation"},{"title":"Activities - Known issues and limitations","url":"https://docs.uipath.com/activities/other/latest/ui-automation/known-issues-and-limitations-citrix"},{"title":"Robotics Process Automation and Citrix...A love Story","url":"https://www.accelirate.com/challenges-of-rpa-in-citrix-environment/"},{"title":"Activities - v23.4","url":"https://docs.uipath.com/activities/other/latest/ui-automation/release-notes-uipath-uiautomation-activities-v23-4"},{"title":"Activities - v24.10","url":"https://docs.uipath.com/activities/other/latest/ui-automation/release-notes-uipath-uiautomation-activities-v24-10"}]},"Based on the search results, here's what I found regarding accessibility tree automation limitations and why computer vision is needed:\n\n## Accessibility Tree and Automation Limitations\n\nTraditional UiPath selectors rely on DOM, accessibility framework, or UI tree, but these approaches have significant constraints. In Citrix or any type of virtualized environment, the controls that RPA is able to use to manipulate the UI via Object IDs are not available.\n\n## Why Computer Vision is Necessary\n\nThe key limitation is that in virtualized environments, the bot is essentially looking at a \"picture,\" so every interaction the bot has will have to be based off of image recognition. UiPath Computer Vision is needed for automating remote desktops, virtual machines, Citrix apps, SAP screens, or image-based interfaces.\n\n## Specific Scenarios Where Computer Vision Applies\n\nDesktop apps that have no UI built with modern accessibility layers, Citrix or VDI without Remote Runtime as interface will be streamed as an image, and RDP sessions without extensions all require computer vision approaches.\n\n## Computer Vision Capabilities\n\nComputer vision breaks limitations by enabling robots to interact with any screen, regardless of technology, environment, or selector availability. UiPath has been ranked by Forrester/Gartner reports as the leader in Citrix/RDP automations, attributed mostly to their industry-leading computer vision capabilities."],"durationSeconds":7.822906709000469,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4470479c-5c0c-4149-9065-e59fdafbf6ab","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"49b8e350-c142-45de-95c0-236e70389a01","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01G9mr3ttWjjW8kf2BnqJctp","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"WinAppDriver deprecated 2025 status Appium Windows driver FlaUI\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Best Desktop Automation Testing Tools in 2026: Use vs Avoid\",\"url\":\"https://testguild.com/automation-tools-desktop/\"},{\"title\":\"UI Automated Testing for Desktop Apps — FlaUI in Practice | KomuraSoft LLC Blog\",\"url\":\"https://comcomponent.com/en/blog/windows-desktop-ui-automation-testing/\"},{\"title\":\"WinAppDriver using deprecated JSON wire protocol · Issue #1610 · microsoft/WinAppDriver\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/microsoft/WinAppDriver/issues/1610\"},{\"title\":\"Windows Tests Running Slow? Maybe Not for Long... - Automate The Planet\",\"url\":\"https://www.automatetheplanet.com/windows-tests-running-slow-maybe-not-for-long/\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - appium/appium-windows-driver: Appium's interface to WindowsAppDriver provided by Microsoft · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/appium/appium-windows-driver\"},{\"title\":\"WinAppDriver to Appium Migration Guide - Automate The Planet\",\"url\":\"https://www.automatetheplanet.com/winappdriver-to-appium-migration-guide/\"},{\"title\":\"git winappdriver\",\"url\":\"https://dpg-shop.schreiner-group.com/by6bl/git-winappdriver.html\"},{\"title\":\"github.com\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/FlaUI/FlaUI.WebDriver\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's the current status of WinAppDriver and related Windows automation tools:\n\n## WinAppDriver Status\n\nMicrosoft hasn't pushed a major release since 2020, with the last stable release (v1.2.1) in November 2020. Effectively, WinAppDriver is dead for new projects, though if you're on WinAppDriver and it's working, it may keep working — but don't start new projects on it.\n\nKey issues include:\n- WinAppDriver server has not been maintained by Microsoft for years\n- Microsoft's WinAppDriver has many open issues, is not actively maintained and not yet open source, and implements the obsolete JSON Wire Protocol by Selenium rather than the new W3C WebDriver standard\n- WinAppDriver has over 1,100 open issues and its server source code was never published\n\n## Alternatives\n\n**Appium Windows Driver:** Appium's Windows Driver docs now point to NovaWindows Driver as the modern replacement. However, Appium is limited in what it can do for Windows automation because it relies entirely on the unmaintained and closed-source WinAppDriver.\n\n**FlaUI:** FlaUI is an MIT-licensed open-source library that is actively maintained, with v5.0.0 released in February 2025. FlaUI is the best tool for WPF automation if your team is in .NET/C#, handling WPF, WinForms, and Win32 well through the UI Automation framework.\n\n**NovaWindows Driver:** NovaWindows Driver by Automate The Planet is recommended as a modern drop-in replacement for Appium Windows Driver.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"c4945de3-0fe2-487d-9d3d-77f55ddf3561","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:40:33.564Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"WinAppDriver deprecated 2025 status Appium Windows driver FlaUI","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01Saov92WW4Uw8V8oQDNDfsX","content":[{"title":"Best Desktop Automation Testing Tools in 2026: Use vs Avoid","url":"https://testguild.com/automation-tools-desktop/"},{"title":"UI Automated Testing for Desktop Apps — FlaUI in Practice | KomuraSoft LLC Blog","url":"https://comcomponent.com/en/blog/windows-desktop-ui-automation-testing/"},{"title":"WinAppDriver using deprecated JSON wire protocol · Issue #1610 · microsoft/WinAppDriver","url":"https://github.com/microsoft/WinAppDriver/issues/1610"},{"title":"Windows Tests Running Slow? Maybe Not for Long... - Automate The Planet","url":"https://www.automatetheplanet.com/windows-tests-running-slow-maybe-not-for-long/"},{"title":"GitHub - appium/appium-windows-driver: Appium's interface to WindowsAppDriver provided by Microsoft · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/appium/appium-windows-driver"},{"title":"WinAppDriver to Appium Migration Guide - Automate The Planet","url":"https://www.automatetheplanet.com/winappdriver-to-appium-migration-guide/"},{"title":"git winappdriver","url":"https://dpg-shop.schreiner-group.com/by6bl/git-winappdriver.html"},{"title":"github.com","url":"https://github.com/FlaUI/FlaUI.WebDriver"}]},"Based on the search results, here's the current status of WinAppDriver and related Windows automation tools:\n\n## WinAppDriver Status\n\nMicrosoft hasn't pushed a major release since 2020, with the last stable release (v1.2.1) in November 2020. Effectively, WinAppDriver is dead for new projects, though if you're on WinAppDriver and it's working, it may keep working — but don't start new projects on it.\n\nKey issues include:\n- WinAppDriver server has not been maintained by Microsoft for years\n- Microsoft's WinAppDriver has many open issues, is not actively maintained and not yet open source, and implements the obsolete JSON Wire Protocol by Selenium rather than the new W3C WebDriver standard\n- WinAppDriver has over 1,100 open issues and its server source code was never published\n\n## Alternatives\n\n**Appium Windows Driver:** Appium's Windows Driver docs now point to NovaWindows Driver as the modern replacement. However, Appium is limited in what it can do for Windows automation because it relies entirely on the unmaintained and closed-source WinAppDriver.\n\n**FlaUI:** FlaUI is an MIT-licensed open-source library that is actively maintained, with v5.0.0 released in February 2025. FlaUI is the best tool for WPF automation if your team is in .NET/C#, handling WPF, WinForms, and Win32 well through the UI Automation framework.\n\n**NovaWindows Driver:** NovaWindows Driver by Automate The Planet is recommended as a modern drop-in replacement for Appium Windows Driver."],"durationSeconds":8.714744374999777,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"49b8e350-c142-45de-95c0-236e70389a01","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"6db3e9ec-fe92-4b02-8c61-990b94f6b3a1","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01C4yMBKrvjRt8C2jkt7NZ5A","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"libei GNOME remote desktop portal input emulation Wayland 2025 automation\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Input emulation on Wayland via `libei` and `RemoteDesktop` portal · rustdesk/rustdesk · Discussion #4515\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/rustdesk/rustdesk/discussions/4515\"},{\"title\":\"libei support · Issue #323 · emersion/xdg-desktop-portal-wlr\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/emersion/xdg-desktop-portal-wlr/issues/323\"},{\"title\":\"Remote Desktop on Wayland in 2025: What Changed for Linux Support Engineers | Stackademic\",\"url\":\"https://stackademic.com/blog/remote-desktop-on-wayland-in-2025-what-changed-for-linux-support-engineers\"},{\"title\":\"Who-T: libei integrations in the XDG RemoteDesktop and InputCapture portals\",\"url\":\"http://who-t.blogspot.com/2026/07/libei-integrations-in-xdg-remotedesktop.html\"},{\"title\":\"xdg-desktop-portal-generic: standalone Wayland XDG Desktop Portal backend (ScreenCast v6) | Lamco Development LLC\",\"url\":\"https://lamco.ai/open-source/xdg-desktop-portal-generic/\"},{\"title\":\"Remote Desktop on Wayland in 2026 - Monodes\",\"url\":\"https://monodes.com/predaelli/2026/02/25/remote-desktop-on-wayland-in-2026/2/\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about libei, GNOME remote desktop portal, input emulation, and Wayland:\n\n## libei Overview\n\nlibei mimics the libinput↔compositor connection, but for emulated events (going from libei client to libeis server/compositor). The RemoteDesktop portal provides applications with a connection to the eis server-part in the compositor.\n\n## Input Emulation on Wayland\n\nlibei enables applications such as RustDesk to implement unprivileged remote access through various Wayland APIs, even if packaged as sandboxed Flatpak. This is significant because traditional X11 methods for input emulation don't work on Wayland.\n\n## Recent Developments (2025-2026)\n\nXwayland can translate XTEST to libei events and facilitate portal interaction, allowing 20-year-old X clients to send pointer and keyboard events through XDG Portal without knowing about it. Clients using XDG portals will work with any compositor implementing the portal, with no need for GNOME/KDE-specific APIs.\n\n## Portal Architecture\n\nxdg-desktop-portal is the Freedesktop spec that lets sandboxed applications ask for privileged capabilities such as remote-desktop input through a permission-mediated D-Bus interface. On Wayland, screen capture/control is mediated by the compositor via PipeWire and xdg-desktop-portal.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"1e1d7949-8792-4bf1-ae39-3e47baac7b3e","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:40:34.280Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"libei GNOME remote desktop portal input emulation Wayland 2025 automation","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_019jXztdKo4cCdtaq8Y4jiPt","content":[{"title":"Input emulation on Wayland via `libei` and `RemoteDesktop` portal · rustdesk/rustdesk · Discussion #4515","url":"https://github.com/rustdesk/rustdesk/discussions/4515"},{"title":"libei support · Issue #323 · emersion/xdg-desktop-portal-wlr","url":"https://github.com/emersion/xdg-desktop-portal-wlr/issues/323"},{"title":"Remote Desktop on Wayland in 2025: What Changed for Linux Support Engineers | Stackademic","url":"https://stackademic.com/blog/remote-desktop-on-wayland-in-2025-what-changed-for-linux-support-engineers"},{"title":"Who-T: libei integrations in the XDG RemoteDesktop and InputCapture portals","url":"http://who-t.blogspot.com/2026/07/libei-integrations-in-xdg-remotedesktop.html"},{"title":"xdg-desktop-portal-generic: standalone Wayland XDG Desktop Portal backend (ScreenCast v6) | Lamco Development LLC","url":"https://lamco.ai/open-source/xdg-desktop-portal-generic/"},{"title":"Remote Desktop on Wayland in 2026 - Monodes","url":"https://monodes.com/predaelli/2026/02/25/remote-desktop-on-wayland-in-2026/2/"}]},"Based on the search results, here's what I found about libei, GNOME remote desktop portal, input emulation, and Wayland:\n\n## libei Overview\n\nlibei mimics the libinput↔compositor connection, but for emulated events (going from libei client to libeis server/compositor). The RemoteDesktop portal provides applications with a connection to the eis server-part in the compositor.\n\n## Input Emulation on Wayland\n\nlibei enables applications such as RustDesk to implement unprivileged remote access through various Wayland APIs, even if packaged as sandboxed Flatpak. This is significant because traditional X11 methods for input emulation don't work on Wayland.\n\n## Recent Developments (2025-2026)\n\nXwayland can translate XTEST to libei events and facilitate portal interaction, allowing 20-year-old X clients to send pointer and keyboard events through XDG Portal without knowing about it. Clients using XDG portals will work with any compositor implementing the portal, with no need for GNOME/KDE-specific APIs.\n\n## Portal Architecture\n\nxdg-desktop-portal is the Freedesktop spec that lets sandboxed applications ask for privileged capabilities such as remote-desktop input through a permission-mediated D-Bus interface. On Wayland, screen capture/control is mediated by the compositor via PipeWire and xdg-desktop-portal."],"durationSeconds":7.718965374998748,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6db3e9ec-fe92-4b02-8c61-990b94f6b3a1","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"cb3ef1e7-68a5-4b6d-8c44-a96edfdd6c70","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DKkd7mEcbvPrmsYE2VTTiY","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"dogtail AT-SPI Wayland GNOME accessibility automation status 2025\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"dogtail · PyPI\",\"url\":\"https://pypi.org/project/dogtail/2.0.dev2/\"},{\"title\":\"dogtail/README.md at master · vhumpa/dogtail\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/vhumpa/dogtail/blob/master/README.md\"},{\"title\":\"[Bug 2158636] [NEW] gnome-shell crashes with SIGSEGV in libatk-bridge/libatspi during heavy dogtail (AT-SPI) UI automation on both X11 and Wayland\",\"url\":\"http://www.mail-archive.com/desktop-bugs@lists.ubuntu.com/msg833183.html\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - splondike/wayland-accessibility-notes: Research on accessibility in the Wayland based Linux desktop · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/splondike/wayland-accessibility-notes\"},{\"title\":\"Wayland features - Fedora Project Wiki\",\"url\":\"https://fedoraproject.org/wiki/Wayland_features\"},{\"title\":\"Automation through Accessibility | GNOME Terminal automation on Fedora 38 with Wayland\",\"url\":\"https://modehnal.github.io/\"},{\"title\":\"Accessibility/Wayland – GNOME Wiki Archive\",\"url\":\"https://wiki.gnome.org/Accessibility/Wayland\"},{\"title\":\"Automation through Accessibility - Fedora Magazine\",\"url\":\"https://fedoramagazine.org/automation-through-accessibility/\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's the current status of dogtail, AT-SPI, Wayland, and GNOME accessibility automation as of 2025:\n\n## Recent Developments\n\nDogtail was released on October 29, 2025, as a GUI test tool and automation framework that uses Accessibility (AT-SPI) technologies to communicate with desktop applications. In June 2025, support for LightDM systems was added to dogtail-run-headless-next, enabling simple session management on primarily non-GNOME distros.\n\n## Wayland Support Implementation\n\nDogtail works great in combination with behave and qecore if you're interested in using it with modern wayland-based GNOME, and should work with any desktop environment that still runs atspi with Xorg. Dogtail now uses the Screen Cast and Remote Desktop API, enabling connection to either a specific window or the entire screen to simulate user input actions.\n\n## Wayland Integration Challenges\n\nWayland does not expose desktop coordinates so gnome-ponytail-daemon uses remote-desktop and screen-cast APIs in order to work around that. Most of what's needed from gnome-ponytail-daemon is hidden behind dogtail, which uses gnome-ponytail-daemon functions when needed, enabling seamless work without the need to change API calls.\n\n## Known Issues\n\nThere are documented stability concerns: Desktop sessions have crashed with \"Something went wrong\" errors during dogtail-based automated UI tests on both X11 and Wayland on Ubuntu 24.04.x, with gnome-shell dumping core with Signal 11 (SEGV).\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"5215287e-e5fb-4f3f-81c0-de397e05a288","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:40:34.569Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"dogtail AT-SPI Wayland GNOME accessibility automation status 2025","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01KuX72rgYqa3qZKErLxgok5","content":[{"title":"dogtail · PyPI","url":"https://pypi.org/project/dogtail/2.0.dev2/"},{"title":"dogtail/README.md at master · vhumpa/dogtail","url":"https://github.com/vhumpa/dogtail/blob/master/README.md"},{"title":"[Bug 2158636] [NEW] gnome-shell crashes with SIGSEGV in libatk-bridge/libatspi during heavy dogtail (AT-SPI) UI automation on both X11 and Wayland","url":"http://www.mail-archive.com/desktop-bugs@lists.ubuntu.com/msg833183.html"},{"title":"GitHub - Fedora Magazine","url":"https://fedoramagazine.org/automation-through-accessibility/"}]},"Based on the search results, here's the current status of dogtail, AT-SPI, Wayland, and GNOME accessibility automation as of 2025:\n\n## Recent Developments\n\nDogtail was released on October 29, 2025, as a GUI test tool and automation framework that uses Accessibility (AT-SPI) technologies to communicate with desktop applications. In June 2025, support for LightDM systems was added to dogtail-run-headless-next, enabling simple session management on primarily non-GNOME distros.\n\n## Wayland Support Implementation\n\nDogtail works great in combination with behave and qecore if you're interested in using it with modern wayland-based GNOME, and should work with any desktop environment that still runs atspi with Xorg. Dogtail now uses the Screen Cast and Remote Desktop API, enabling connection to either a specific window or the entire screen to simulate user input actions.\n\n## Wayland Integration Challenges\n\nWayland does not expose desktop coordinates so gnome-ponytail-daemon uses remote-desktop and screen-cast APIs in order to work around that. Most of what's needed from gnome-ponytail-daemon is hidden behind dogtail, which uses gnome-ponytail-daemon functions when needed, enabling seamless work without the need to change API calls.\n\n## Known Issues\n\nThere are documented stability concerns: Desktop sessions have crashed with \"Something went wrong\" errors during dogtail-based automated UI tests on both X11 and Wayland on Ubuntu 24.04.x, with gnome-shell dumping core with Signal 11 (SEGV)."],"durationSeconds":8.345833374999463,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"cb3ef1e7-68a5-4b6d-8c44-a96edfdd6c70","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}

trycua/cua: Scale computer-use 2.0 with open-source drivers, cross-OS fleets, and benchmarks for training, evaluation, and data generation. · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/trycua/cua\"},{\"title\":\"www.ycombinator.com\",\"url\":\"https://www.ycombinator.com/companies/cua\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about these technologies:\n\n## Bytebot\n\nBytebot is a self-hosted AI desktop agent that automates computer tasks through natural language commands, operating within a containerized Linux desktop environment.\n\n## Cua (Computer-Use Agent) Framework\n\ntrycua open-sourced CUA, a comprehensive infrastructure for Computer-Use Agents, under an MIT Licence. Cua is an open-source infrastructure platform created by trycua that enables developers to build, benchmark, and deploy AI agents capable of controlling computers autonomously.\n\nThe release includes Cua Driver for background macOS application control, an OS-agnostic Cua SDK providing sandboxes for Linux, macOS, Windows, and Android, and CuaBot for multi-agent sandbox orchestration.\n\n## Desktop Sandbox & Computer Use\n\nE2B Desktop sandboxes allow AI agents to see, understand, and control virtual Linux desktops. Cua offers isolated, self-hostable sandboxes powered by Docker, QEMU, and Apple Virtualization.framework, allowing you to spin up pristine macOS, Linux, or Windows environments on demand, run your agents, capture every interaction, and tear down the sandbox without affecting your host system.\n\n## Agent Execution\n\nThe Cua driver ships with a CLI and an MCP server, so Claude Code, Cursor, and any other MCP-aware agent can drive native macOS apps with a few tool calls.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"3a561af7-122f-43ca-919a-2b119c95cf02","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:40:51.766Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"Bytebot cua trycua e2b desktop sandbox computer use agent execution 2025","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01PiHRWbR8W74MCJqGAHN4wG","content":[{"title":"cua · GitHub Topics · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/topics/cua"},{"title":"How Manus Uses E2B to Provide Agents With Virtual Computers — E2B Blog","url":"https://e2b.dev/blog/how-manus-uses-e2b-to-provide-agents-with-virtual-computers"},{"title":"Computer use","url":"https://e2b.dev/docs/use-cases/computer-use"},{"title":"trycua Open-Sources CUA Agent Framework","url":"https://pulse24.ai/news/2026/4/29/13/trycua-open-sources-cua-agent-framework"},{"title":"Cua: Build Powerful AI Agents That Control Computers - {"parentUuid":"5f413481-c532-4b0a-8361-abddeac6ebd5","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XYtvLRniyxPB4C11jq4pfS","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"mediar-ai terminator desktop automation rust AI agents screenpipe\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"mediar-ai/terminator | DeepWiki\",\"url\":\"https://deepwiki.com/mediar-ai/terminator\"},{\"title\":\"mediar · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/mediar-ai\"},{\"title\":\"Screenpipe MCP Server: The Ultimate Guide to Giving AI Vision and Memory on Your Desktop\",\"url\":\"https://skywork.ai/skypage/en/screenpipe-mcp-ai-vision-memory/1978719521292800000\"},{\"title\":\"terminator-rs - crates.io: Rust Package Registry\",\"url\":\"https://crates.io/crates/terminator-rs\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - mediar-ai/terminator: playwright for windows computer use · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/mediar-ai/terminator\"},{\"title\":\"Automate repetitive desktop workflows with AI | Mediar\",\"url\":\"https://www.mediar.ai/t/mediar-github\"},{\"title\":\"github- terminator :Features,Alternatives | Toolerific\",\"url\":\"https://toolerific.ai/ai-tools/opensource/mediar-ai-terminator\"},{\"title\":\"DEV Community\",\"url\":\"https://dev.to/githubopensource/terminator-the-ai-powered-desktop-automation-revolution-4f1m\"},{\"title\":\"www.g2.com\",\"url\":\"https://www.g2.com/products/screenpi/discuss\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about these technologies:\n\n## Terminator (mediar-ai)\n\nTerminator is a Windows desktop automation system that enables programmatic control of desktop applications through accessibility APIs, with native AI integration via the Model Context Protocol (MCP). The system provides deterministic UI automation capabilities combined with AI-powered recovery mechanisms, achieving >95% success rates for GUI automation tasks.\n\nIt creates automations that work across any desktop app or browser, runs 100x faster than ChatGPT Agents, Claude, Perplexity Comet, BrowserBase, and BrowserUse, and achieves a 95% success rate.\n\nThe project has several components:\n- A Rust crate at the core\n- A Model Context Protocol server so Claude, Cursor, or VS Code can drive the desktop\n- A command-line entry point for running and scripting workflows, and a workflow package for composing multi-step desktop automations\n\n## Screenpipe\n\nScreenpipe MCP server exposes tools to AI agents for memory/retrieval through semantic search across recorded history, allowing queries for OCR text, audio transcriptions, and UI elements that appeared on screen. It provides action/automation tools including OS-level control primitives that give AI \"hands\" to act on desktop, navigate applications, open apps, and type text.\n\nBoth tools work together as part of the AI automation ecosystem, with Screenpipe providing memory and vision capabilities while Terminator provides desktop control.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"e4bccff8-de80-43b8-aed6-01255529d680","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:40:51.911Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"mediar-ai terminator desktop automation rust AI agents screenpipe","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_0137Y7BWw2Ajb2634pngcrk4","content":[{"title":"mediar-ai/terminator | DeepWiki","url":"https://deepwiki.com/mediar-ai/terminator"},{"title":"mediar · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/mediar-ai"},{"title":"Screenpipe MCP Server: The Ultimate Guide to Giving AI Vision and Memory on Your Desktop","url":"https://skywork.ai/skypage/en/screenpipe-mcp-ai-vision-memory/1978719521292800000"},{"title":"terminator-rs - crates.io: Rust Package Registry","url":"https://crates.io/crates/terminator-rs"},{"title":"GitHub - mediar-ai/terminator: playwright for windows computer use · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/mediar-ai/terminator"},{"title":"Automate repetitive desktop workflows with AI | Mediar","url":"https://www.mediar.ai/t/mediar-github"},{"title":"github- terminator :Features,Alternatives | Toolerific","url":"https://toolerific.ai/ai-tools/opensource/mediar-ai-terminator"},{"title":"DEV Community","url":"https://dev.to/githubopensource/terminator-the-ai-powered-desktop-automation-revolution-4f1m"},{"title":"www.g2.com","url":"https://www.g2.com/products/screenpi/discuss"}]},"Based on the search results, here's what I found about these technologies:\n\n## Terminator (mediar-ai)\n\nTerminator is a Windows desktop automation system that enables programmatic control of desktop applications through accessibility APIs, with native AI integration via the Model Context Protocol (MCP). The system provides deterministic UI automation capabilities combined with AI-powered recovery mechanisms, achieving >95% success rates for GUI automation tasks.\n\nIt creates automations that work across any desktop app or browser, runs 100x faster than ChatGPT Agents, Claude, Perplexity Comet, BrowserBase, and BrowserUse, and achieves a 95% success rate.\n\nThe project has several components:\n- A Rust crate at the core\n- A Model Context Protocol server so Claude, Cursor, or VS Code can drive the desktop\n- A command-line entry point for running and scripting workflows, and a workflow package for composing multi-step desktop automations\n\n## Screenpipe\n\nScreenpipe MCP server exposes tools to AI agents for memory/retrieval through semantic search across recorded history, allowing queries for OCR text, audio transcriptions, and UI elements that appeared on screen. It provides action/automation tools including OS-level control primitives that give AI \"hands\" to act on desktop, navigate applications, open apps, and type text.\n\nBoth tools work together as part of the AI automation ecosystem, with Screenpipe providing memory and vision capabilities while Terminator provides desktop control."],"durationSeconds":8.544777541998775,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5f413481-c532-4b0a-8361-abddeac6ebd5","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}

in computer use agent benchmarking:\n\n## UI-TARS Agent S2\n\nAgent-S2 incorporates specialist grounding (Mixture-of-Grounding) and Proactive Hierarchical Planning, achieving strong performance across OSWorld, WindowsAgentArena, and AndroidWorld.\n\n## OSWorld Benchmark\n\nOSWorld is a benchmark consisting of 369 real-world computer use tasks across the following categories: OS, Office (LibreOffice Calc, Impress, Writer), Daily (Chrome, VLC Player, Thunderbird), Professional (VS Code and GIMP) and Workflow (tasks involving multiple apps).\n\n## PyAutoGUI Action Execution\n\nOSWorld supports two action spaces: pyautogui and computer_13, with pyautogui being primarily adopted as it eliminates the need for additional labels to define the action space when formulating prompts and is widely employed by mainstream baseline methods.\n\nPyAutoGUI is an open-source, cross-platform Python module designed for programmatic control of the mouse and keyboard, enabling the execution of basic actions, mouse clicks, and keyboard input operations.\n\nThe search results indicate that these components are interconnected parts of the broader computer use agent evaluation landscape, with Here's what the results show:\n\n## Wayland Automation Challenges\n\nAutomating keyboard and mouse input on Wayland is notoriously difficult due to security restrictions. While X11 users had xdotool, Wayland users rely on ydotool.\n\n## ydotool Difficulties\n\nGetting the ydotoold daemon to start automatically with the correct permissions can be a headache. ydotool and ydotoold need root permissions to access the devices, and running ydotool with sudo is not practical and hardly feasible in very likely circumstances, such as in scripts launched from shortcut keys.\n\nThe search results reveal that users face various technical barriers when trying to automate desktop input on Wayland, with ydotool being a common but frustrating solution due to permission and daemon management issues. However, the results don't contain specific Reddit discussions matching your complete search phrase. {"parentUuid":"0f2b8877-2edc-4396-81da-67a68a28d089","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VNtLu2yhsM9SUU4JUMzJqF","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"Maestro mobile UI testing declarative YAML flows desktop support\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Beginner's Guide to Maestro Mobile UI Testing | Bug Tracking Blog @ Bird Eats Bug\",\"url\":\"https://birdeatsbug.com/blog/maestro-testing\"},{\"title\":\"Maestro for Real-Time UI Validation\",\"url\":\"https://maestro.dev/insights/maestro-for-real-time-ui-validation\"},{\"title\":\"End-to-End UI Testing for Mobile Apps with Maestro\",\"url\":\"https://maestro.dev/insights/end-to-end-ui-testing-for-mobile-apps-with-maestro\"},{\"title\":\"Maestro: Next-Generation Mobile UI Testing Framework (Appium alternative?) | by Sahil Tiwari | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/@sahiltiwari_66376/maestro-next-generation-mobile-ui-testing-framework-appium-alternative-fcd3c4727771\"},{\"title\":\"Introducing: Maestro — Painless Mobile UI Automation | by Leland Takamine | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/@leland_40592/introducing-maestro-painless-mobile-ui-automation-bee4992d13c1\"},{\"title\":\"Maestro Mobile UI Testing Guide: YAML Flows for Android & iOS | QASkills.sh\",\"url\":\"https://qaskills.sh/blog/maestro-mobile-ui-testing-guide\"},{\"title\":\"Maestro Mobile Testing: The Complete YAML-Based Guide for 2026 | QASkills.sh\",\"url\":\"https://qaskills.sh/blog/maestro-mobile-testing-guide-2026\"},{\"title\":\"glama.ai\",\"url\":\"https://glama.ai/mcp/servers/mobile-dev-inc/Maestro\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about Maestro mobile UI testing:\n\n## Overview\n\nMaestro is an open-source mobile UI automation framework designed for simplicity and effectiveness. It works by interpreting YAML test files and running them against connected devices or emulators.\n\n## Declarative YAML Flows\n\nMaestro defines test flows in a declarative format where each step describes user actions such as tapping, typing, or navigating, making scripts easier to read and update. Maestro's YAML syntax is designed to be easy to use, relying on plain language commands like tapOn, inputText, and assertVisible.\n\n## Desktop and Cross-Platform Support\n\nMaestro works on Android devices/emulators, iOS simulators, and desktop/mobile web browsers without extra setup. Maestro runs on iOS and Android and supports ReactNative, Flutter, WebViews, and pure Native applications.\n\n## Key Features\n\n- It enables teams to define test flows in YAML, making them human-readable and easier to maintain, while still supporting advanced actions such as gestures, deep links, and biometrics.\n- Every command waits for the UI to settle and retries automatically, and you almost never write explicit sleeps or waits.\n- Maestro Studio builds on this ease of use by offering a visual, no-code interface where users can record actions, inspect elements, and generate test flows without needing to touch the command line.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"ce048a63-9bb4-400c-9642-2aa6ca1b6f8c","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:41:11.899Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"Maestro mobile UI testing declarative YAML flows desktop here's what I found about Maestro mobile UI testing:\n\n## Overview\n\nMaestro is an open-source mobile UI automation framework designed for simplicity and effectiveness. It works by interpreting YAML test files and running them against connected devices or emulators.\n\n## Declarative YAML Flows\n\nMaestro defines test flows in a declarative format where each step describes user actions such as tapping, typing, or navigating, making scripts easier to read and update. Maestro's YAML syntax is designed to be easy to use, relying on plain language commands like tapOn, inputText, and assertVisible.\n\n## Desktop and Cross-Platform Support\n\nMaestro works on Android devices/emulators, iOS simulators, and desktop/mobile web browsers without extra setup. Maestro runs on iOS and Android and supports ReactNative, Flutter, WebViews, and pure Native applications.\n\n## Key Features\n\n- It enables teams to define test flows in YAML, making them human-readable and easier to maintain, while still supporting advanced actions such as gestures, deep links, and biometrics.\n- Every command waits for the UI to settle and retries automatically, and you almost never write explicit sleeps or waits.\n- Maestro Studio builds on this ease of use by offering a visual, no-code interface where users can record actions, inspect elements, and generate test flows without needing to touch the command line."],"durationSeconds":7.8363912090007215,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0f2b8877-2edc-4396-81da-67a68a28d089","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"08c1a932-af48-4ca2-915d-3944ef593204","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LseQrWyJjSoqs77pf5bmth","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"computer use agent reliability clicks wrong coordinates deterministic replay guardrails 2025\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Claude Computer Use Proxy Setup Guide 2026 | Coronium.io\",\"url\":\"https://www.coronium.io/blog/claude-computer-use-proxy\"},{\"title\":\"Visual Confused Deputy: Exploiting and Defending Perception Failures in Computer-Using Agents\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.14707\"},{\"title\":\"Trustworthy AI Agents: Deterministic Replay | Sakura Sky: Cloud, Data, Security\",\"url\":\"https://www.sakurasky.com/blog/missing-primitives-for-trustworthy-ai-part-8/\"},{\"title\":\"Chapter 28: Computer Use | AI Agent Architecture\",\"url\":\"https://waylandz.com/ai-agent-book-en/chapter-28-computer-use/\"},{\"title\":\"AI Agent Guardrails: The Replicant Approach to Reliable AI\",\"url\":\"https://www.replicant.com/blog/ai-agent-guardrails-the-replicant-approach\"},{\"title\":\"Improve complex UI automation with computer‑using agents | Microsoft Copilot Blog\",\"url\":\"https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-copilot/blog/copilot-studio/computer-using-agents-now-deliver-more-secure-ui-automation-at-scale/\"},{\"title\":\"MirrorGuard: Toward Secure Computer-Use Agents via Simulation-to-Real Reasoning Correction\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.12822\"},{\"title\":\"On the Reliability of Computer Use Agents\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/html/2604.17849v1\"},{\"title\":\"Replayable Agent Runs: The Debugging Trick That Ships | by Thinking Loop | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/@ThinkingLoop/replayable-agent-runs-the-debugging-trick-that-ships-f5460ebf390a\"}]\n\nI found relevant search results covering multiple aspects of your query. Here's what the current research and industry approaches show:\n\n## Agent Reliability and Wrong Coordinates\n\nRecent benchmarks consistently show that computer-using agents remain unreliable in realistic GUI environments. ScreenSpot-Pro isolates the visual grounding problem and shows that accurate target selection on professional GUIs remains difficult even for strong models. Specifically, agents may misread the UI, click the wrong control, or encounter hidden pop-ups.\n\n## Deterministic Replay\n\nEvery action is logged as a deterministic tool call with typed inputs (coordinates, keystrokes, scroll deltas), making Computer Use runs fully replayable and auditable, critical for regulated workflows. Step-by-step action logs with action types, coordinates, timestamps, and context show exactly what the agent saw and did, pinpointing where the UI changed.\n\n## Guardrails Approaches\n\nThere are multiple guardrail strategies being developed:\n\n- **Verification guardrails**: A guardrail runs after the agent proposes an action but before execution, consisting of two lightweight embedding classifications: a visual check of the click-target crop and a textual check of the agent's reasoning. The total latency is approximately 14 ms per click on an AMD MI300X GPU.\n\n- **Deterministic execution model**: The single most important principle is not letting an LLM make decisions you don't need an LLM for, using LLM-based execution for flexibility and deterministic execution for correctness and predictability.\n\n- **Strategy-level determinism**: Agents can be prompted to generate a plan that is reused in future repeated runs rather than allowing replanning on each execution.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"ede9c27c-c092-4957-83d8-d10714b33956","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:41:13.089Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"computer use agent reliability clicks wrong coordinates deterministic {"parentUuid":"545655bb-5315-418f-ba3a-598c4b1c1b1d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012ok58bYihPG11v2wzcjeUa","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"Electron desktop app end-to-end testing CI 2025 Playwright tauri-driver WebDriver\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Testing Tauri desktop apps - writing end-to-end tests in Rust for Desktop GUIs · tauri-apps · Discussion #3768\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/orgs/tauri-apps/discussions/3768\"},{\"title\":\"Testing Tauri desktop apps - writing end-to-end tests in Rust for Desktop GUIs · tauri-apps/tauri · Discussion #3768\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/discussions/3768\"},{\"title\":\"Automated Testing | Electron\",\"url\":\"https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/automated-testing\"},{\"title\":\"Playwright Testing: WebKit Engine Mismatch | zudo-tauri-wisdom\",\"url\":\"https://takazudomodular.com/pj/zudo-tauri/docs/frontend/playwright-engine-pitfall/\"},{\"title\":\"Tests | Tauri\",\"url\":\"https://v2.tauri.app/develop/tests/\"},{\"title\":\"Tauri Apps - TestDriver AI\",\"url\":\"https://docs.testdriver.ai/v6/apps/tauri-apps\"},{\"title\":\"github.com\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/topheman/webassembly-component-model-experiments/pull/5\"},{\"title\":\"Software Alternatives, Accelerators & Startups\",\"url\":\"https://www.saashub.com/alternatives/post-news-ycombinator-2022-10-01-testing-react-apps-in-2022-with-cypress-an-in-depth-guide-for-beginners-1526119\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found regarding end-to-end testing for desktop applications in 2025:\n\n## Key Testing Approaches\n\n**For Tauri Applications:**\nTauri supports the webdriver interface on Windows and Linux through tauri-driver, with examples and documentation for WebdriverIO and Selenium. Tauri provides support for end-to-end testing utilizing the WebDriver protocol, with WebdriverIO testing supporting Windows, Linux, and macOS.\n\nTauri's official testing docs recommend WebDriver via tauri-driver for real desktop end-to-end testing, which targets the actual desktop app, not a standalone browser tab. However, tauri-driver is still labeled \"pre-alpha\" and should be treated as early-stage infrastructure.\n\n**For Electron Applications:**\nPlaywright has experimental Electron support via Electron's support for the Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP) and launches your app in development mode through the _electron.launch API.\n\n**Important Consideration for Cross-Platform Testing:**\nTauri does not use the same rendering engine on every platform—on Windows it uses WebView2 (Chromium-based), but on macOS it uses WKWebView (WebKit) and on Linux it uses WebKitGTK.\n\n## CI/CD Integration\n\nTauri offers tauri-action to help run GitHub actions, but any sort of CI/CD runner can be used with Tauri as long as each platform has the required libraries installed to compile against.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"1bc81ff7-6098-4181-b5bc-599ab333374b","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:41:13.109Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"Electron desktop app end-to-end testing CI 2025 Playwright tauri-driver WebDriver","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01337n8BBC82fSq46LE8G4Pe","content":[{"title":"Testing Tauri desktop apps - Tauri provides support for end-to-end testing utilizing the WebDriver protocol, with WebdriverIO testing supporting Windows, Linux, and macOS.\n\nTauri's official testing docs recommend WebDriver via tauri-driver for real desktop end-to-end testing, which targets the actual desktop app, not a standalone browser tab. {"parentUuid":"a5753448-2d67-42f0-960b-0ab2a1eb033d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01J1DbR1hdmu6MMQo3Ccjdxt","type":"tool_result","content":"# Terminator: Desktop Automation Framework\n\n## Overview\nTerminator is a computer use tool that gives AI assistants (Claude, Cursor, VS Code) the ability to control desktops and automate tasks across applications.\n\n## Platform Support\n**Windows only** — macOS and Linux are not currently supported.\n\n## Element Location Method\nTerminator locates UI elements through the **Windows Accessibility Tree**, using selectors like `name:Seven`, `role:Button`, and `window:Calculator`. The recommended inspection tool is [Accessibility Insights for Windows](https://accessibilityinsights.io/downloads/).\n\n## License\n**MIT-licensed** — permitting forking and deployment without vendor lock-in.\n\n## Language Bindings\n- Python (`terminator.py` - partial support)\n- TypeScript (`@mediar-ai/terminator`)\n- Rust (`terminator-rs`)\n- Workflow format (TypeScript/YAML via `@mediar-ai/workflow`)\n\n## Workflow Format\nWorkflows can be recorded as deterministic code in TypeScript/YAML, with \"NodeJS SDK in YAML workflows\" and \"OS event recording → YAML generation\" capabilities.\n\n## Maintenance Activity\nActive development: 2,941 commits on main branch; 1.6k stars; recent updates (January 2026); raised $2.8M in funding.\n\n## Deterministic Workflows\nThe project emphasizes pre-training \"workflows as deterministic code, and calling AI only when recovery is needed,\" achieving \">95% success rate.\""}]},"uuid":"01c0e182-9183-466a-85a3-51ec859a1727","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:41:35.408Z","toolUseResult":{"bytes":371186,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# Terminator: Desktop Automation Framework\n\n## Overview\nTerminator is a computer use tool that gives AI assistants (Claude, Cursor, VS Code) the ability to control desktops and automate tasks across applications.\n\n## Platform Support\n**Windows only** — macOS and Linux are not currently supported.\n\n## Element Location Method\nTerminator locates UI elements through the **Windows Accessibility Tree**, using selectors like `name:Seven`, `role:Button`, and `window:Calculator`. The recommended inspection tool is [Accessibility Insights for Windows](https://accessibilityinsights.io/downloads/).\n\n## License\n**MIT-licensed** — permitting forking and deployment without vendor lock-in.\n\n## Language Bindings\n- Python (`terminator.py` - partial support)\n- TypeScript (`@mediar-ai/terminator`)\n- Rust (`terminator-rs`)\n- Workflow format (TypeScript/YAML via `@mediar-ai/workflow`)\n\n## Workflow Format\nWorkflows can be recorded as deterministic code in TypeScript/YAML, with \"NodeJS SDK in YAML workflows\" and \"OS event recording → YAML generation\" capabilities.\n\n## Maintenance Activity\nActive development: 2,941 commits on main branch; 1.6k stars; recent updates (January 2026); raised $2.8M in funding.\n\n## Deterministic Workflows\nThe project emphasizes pre-training \"workflows as deterministic code, and calling AI only when recovery is needed,\" achieving \">95% success rate.\"","durationMs":6055,"url":"https://github.com/mediar-ai/terminator"},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a5753448-2d67-42f0-960b-0ab2a1eb033d","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"a2c13c18-af1a-4b5b-b238-a9a79c56f143","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01GVD2ZY3AYMzUrCKKgfQ25M","type":"tool_result","content":"# Enigo: Cross-Platform Input Simulation Library\n\n## Platform Support\n\nEnigo supports Windows, macOS, and Linux (X11) by default. Linux support extends to Wayland and libei through experimental feature flags. The library also theoretically works on *BSDs with X11/Wayland, though this lacks active testing infrastructure.\n\n**Wayland Implementation:** The project offers experimental Wayland protocol support hidden behind feature flags, alongside libei mouse and text simulation. However, \"there are currently some bugs with it,\" indicating the Wayland implementation requires caution for production use.\n\n## Maintenance Status\n\nThe repository shows active development with 853 commits on the main branch and 10 open pull requests. The project maintains a changelog documenting version migrations, though the web content doesn't specify the latest release date or version number explicitly.\n\n## Licensing\n\nThe project uses the MIT license, allowing broad commercial and private use with minimal restrictions.\n\n## Known Limitations\n\n- Linux users need libxdo-dev installed for the xdo feature\n- \"Some platforms have security measures in place to prevent programs from entering keys or controlling the mouse\"\n- Tests must run sequentially to avoid interference\n- Windows uses physical pixels rather than logical pixels for coordinates\n\n## Usage & Community\n\nThe library includes serialization/deserialization capabilities, comprehensive examples, and documentation. With 1.8k GitHub stars and 153 forks, it demonstrates reasonable community adoption, though specific end-user examples aren't detailed in the provided content."}]},"uuid":"fad227f9-6567-42e7-9959-1f4df7e1994a","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:41:36.276Z","toolUseResult":{"bytes":312112,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# Enigo: Cross-Platform Input Simulation Library\n\n## Platform Support\n\nEnigo supports Windows, macOS, and Linux (X11) by default. Linux support extends to Wayland and libei through experimental feature flags. The library also theoretically works on *BSDs with X11/Wayland, though this lacks active testing infrastructure.\n\n**Wayland Implementation:** The project offers experimental Wayland protocol support hidden behind feature flags, alongside libei mouse and text simulation. However, \"there are currently some bugs with it,\" indicating the Wayland implementation requires caution for production use.\n\n## Maintenance Status\n\nThe repository shows active development with 853 commits on the main branch and 10 open pull requests. The project maintains a changelog documenting version migrations, though the web content doesn't specify the latest release date or version number explicitly.\n\n## Licensing\n\nThe project uses the MIT license, allowing broad commercial and private use with minimal restrictions.\n\n## Known Limitations\n\n- Linux users need libxdo-dev installed for the xdo feature\n- \"Some platforms have security measures in place to prevent programs from entering keys or controlling the mouse\"\n- Tests must run sequentially to avoid interference\n- Windows uses physical pixels rather than logical pixels for coordinates\n\n## Usage & Community\n\nThe library includes serialization/deserialization capabilities, comprehensive examples, and documentation. With 1.8k GitHub stars and 153 forks, it demonstrates reasonable community adoption, though specific end-user examples aren't detailed in the provided content.","durationMs":5416,"url":"https://github.com/enigo-rs/enigo"},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a2c13c18-af1a-4b5b-b238-a9a79c56f143","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"82c7aae1-797c-4b39-b1bc-a254b01b6e8a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EVCdemSaZTFmxjQepvWTTr","type":"tool_result","content":"# agent-desktop Overview\n\n## What It Is\n\n**agent-desktop** is a native desktop automation CLI built in Rust designed specifically for AI agents. It provides structured access to applications through OS accessibility trees rather than screenshots or pixel matching.\n\n## Platforms & Support\n\nCurrently supports **macOS 13.0+**. Windows and Linux support are listed as \"Planned\" for most features including accessibility trees, input methods, screenshots, and app management.\n\n## How It Works\n\nThe tool operates through two primary mechanisms:\n\n1. **Accessibility Trees**: \"Control any application through OS accessibility trees with structured JSON output and deterministic element refs.\" Rather than visual analysis, it reads the semantic structure applications expose via accessibility APIs.\n\n2. **Reference System**: Elements receive qualified references like `@s8f3k2p9:e1`. This enables precise, repeatable targeting without coordinates.\n\n3. **Progressive Traversal**: For dense applications, \"progressive skeleton traversal\" achieves \"78–96% token reduction on dense apps\" by starting with shallow overviews and drilling into regions of interest.\n\n## CLI Shape\n\nThe tool offers 58 command names across categories: observation (`snapshot`, `screenshot`, `find`), interaction (`click`, `type`, `scroll`), keyboard/mouse, app management, notifications, clipboard, and sessions. Output is consistently structured JSON.\n\n## Output Format\n\nAll responses use a versioned JSON envelope with error codes and exit codes—\"machine-readable responses with error codes and recovery hints.\"\n\n## License & Maintenance\n\n**Apache-2.0** licensed. The repository shows active maintenance (979 stars, 63 forks, 4 open pull requests, recent CI passes).\n\n## Intended Users\n\nDesigned for AI agents automating desktop workflows, though humans can use it directly via CLI or FFI bindings (Python, Swift, Go, Ruby, Node, C)."}]},"uuid":"c4b77404-5376-4403-a93b-9a0bfc6951a3","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:41:37.373Z","toolUseResult":{"bytes":498736,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# agent-desktop Overview\n\n## What It Is\n\n**agent-desktop** is a native desktop automation CLI built in Rust designed specifically for AI agents. It provides structured access to applications through OS accessibility trees rather than screenshots or pixel matching.\n\n## Platforms & Support\n\nCurrently supports **macOS 13.0+**. Windows and Linux support are listed as \"Planned\" for most features including accessibility trees, input methods, screenshots, and app management.\n\n## How It Works\n\nThe tool operates through two primary mechanisms:\n\n1. **Accessibility Trees**: \"Control any application through OS accessibility trees with structured JSON output and deterministic element refs.\" Rather than visual analysis, it reads the semantic structure applications expose via accessibility APIs.\n\n2. **Reference System**: Elements receive qualified references like `@s8f3k2p9:e1`. This enables precise, repeatable targeting without coordinates.\n\n3. **Progressive Traversal**: For dense applications, \"progressive skeleton traversal\" achieves \"78–96% token reduction on dense apps\" by starting with shallow overviews and drilling into regions of interest.\n\n## CLI Shape\n\nThe tool offers 58 command names across categories: observation (`snapshot`, `screenshot`, `find`), interaction (`click`, `type`, `scroll`), keyboard/mouse, app management, notifications, clipboard, and sessions. Output is consistently structured JSON.\n\n## Output Format\n\nAll responses use a versioned JSON envelope with error codes and exit codes—\"machine-readable responses with error codes and recovery hints.\"\n\n## License & Maintenance\n\n**Apache-2.0** licensed. The repository shows active maintenance (979 stars, 63 forks, 4 open pull requests, recent CI passes).\n\n## Intended Users\n\nDesigned for AI agents automating desktop workflows, though humans can use it directly via CLI or FFI bindings (Python, Swift, Go, Ruby, Node, C).","durationMs":7587,"url":"https://github.com/lahfir/agent-desktop"},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"82c7aae1-797c-4b39-b1bc-a254b01b6e8a","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}

When using Wayland, Espanso uses the EVDEVInjector to handle keyboard input injection.\n\n## Capability Requirements\n\nEspanso supports adding the CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE capability to the binary's set of Permitted ones, which grants Espanso the permissions to read and write to any file in the system, but only when explicitly activated by the binary itself. This is the recommended secure approach instead of running Espanso as root.\n\n## Wayland Support Status\n\nWayland support is currently experimental, therefore some features might be missing or not working well yet. Some known limitations on Wayland include keyboard layout requirements and compatibility issues with specific applications and desktop environments.\n\nThe search results show that there are ongoing discussions about proper capability handling, particularly on systems like NixOS where the standard setcap approach needs workarounds for the capabilities to be properly propagated to the Espanso worker processes.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown here's what I found about Espanso's Wayland EVDEV injection:\n\n## How EVDEV Injection Works with Wayland\n\nEspanso requires access to the /dev/input/eventX and /dev/uinput interfaces to detect triggers and inject expansions respectively. When using Wayland, Espanso uses the EVDEVInjector to handle keyboard input injection.\n\n## Capability Requirements\n\nEspanso supports adding the CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE capability to the binary's set of Permitted ones, which grants Espanso the permissions to read and write to any file in the system, but only when explicitly activated by the binary itself. This is the recommended secure approach instead of running Espanso as root.\n\n## Wayland Support Status\n\nWayland support is currently experimental, therefore some features might be missing or not working well yet. Some known limitations on Wayland include keyboard layout requirements and compatibility issues with specific applications and desktop environments.\n\nThe search results show that there are ongoing discussions about proper capability handling, particularly on systems like NixOS where the standard setcap approach needs workarounds for the capabilities to be properly propagated to the Espanso worker processes."],"durationSeconds":6.429224790999666,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"40c1dfed-a967-4cd9-b9c7-2c7559174259","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}

openQA\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/os-autoinst/os-autoinst-needles-openQA\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about openQA, os-autoinst needles, JSON, screenshot regions, and template matching:\n\n## Overview\n\nopenQA checks the presence of elements in a virtual machine's screen using fuzzy image matching between the screen and so-called 'needles'.\n\n## Needle Structure\n\nA needle consists of a full screenshot in PNG format and a JSON file with the same name containing the associated data, like which areas inside the full screenshot are relevant or a list of tags.\n\n## JSON File Format\n\nThe JSON file contains areas with properties including xpos, ypos, width, height, type (match/ocr/exclude), match similarity percentage (0-100), and optional click points, along with tags.\n\n## Area Regions\n\nA needle is comprised of one or more areas, where each area is a matching region inside the associated PNG file; for a needle to match all its areas must match the QEMU screen, and areas have x and y coordinates, dimensional width and height, a type attribute (typically \"match\"), and a match percentage.\n\n## Testing Process\n\nos-autoinst takes screenshots of the QEMU screen and then fuzzy compares them to needles (expected screen content) to satisfy assertions made in the test case.\n\n## Creating Needles\n\nThe easiest way to create or update a needle is to use the openQA needle editor; after defining required tags and areas to match and pressing save, PNG and JSON files are created in the needles directory.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"0fe97122-f4c8-47ea-84d3-6481b702e1da","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:42:15.335Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"openQA os-autoinst needles JSON screenshot regions template matching We forge.\",\"url\":\"https://code.brothertec.eu/simono41/dotool\"},{\"title\":\"python uinput\",\"url\":\"https://pypi.org/project/python-uinput/\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found about dotool:\n\n## Overview\n\ndotool reads actions from stdin and simulates keyboard/mouse input using Linux's uinput module. It works systemwide and supports keyboard layouts.\n\n## Key Features\n\n- **Cross-platform compatibility**: It works system-wide, including in X11, Wayland and TTYs.\n- **Developed by**: The developer is John Gebbie and it's licensed under GNU General Public License v3.0.\n- **Language**: dotool is written in Go.\n\n## Available Commands\n\nAccording to the documentation, dotool supports various input commands including:\n- *mouseto* - Jump the cursor to the position X Y, where X and Y are percentages between 0.0 and 1.0.\n- *mousemove* - Move the cursor relative to its current position.\n- *keydelay*, *keyhold*, *typedelay*, *typehold* - Set the delay between/holding each key.\n\n## Repository\n\nThe official repository is hosted on SourceHut at git.sr.ht/~geb/dotool, with mirrors available on GitHub.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"a8dddd71-5afc-44a9-9e2e-3446e30a6dea","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:42:36.878Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"\"dotool\" uinput sourcehut geb keyboard mouse stdin commands","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01WMHRswYDvh54e9m7UVRuvU","content":[{"title":"~geb/dotool - We forge.","url":"https://code.brothertec.eu/simono41/dotool"},{"title":"python uinput","url":"https://pypi.org/project/python-uinput/"}]},"Based on the search results, here's what I found about dotool:\n\n## Overview\n\ndotool reads actions from stdin and simulates keyboard/mouse input using Linux's uinput module. It works systemwide and supports keyboard layouts.\n\n## Key Features\n\n- **Cross-platform compatibility**: It works system-wide, including in X11, Wayland and TTYs.\n- **Developed by**: The developer is John Gebbie and it's licensed under GNU General Public License v3.0.\n- **Language**: dotool is written in Go.\n\n## Available Commands\n\nAccording to the documentation, dotool supports various input commands including:\n- *mouseto* - Jump the cursor to the position X Y, where X and Y are percentages between 0.0 and 1.0.\n- *mousemove* - Move the cursor relative to its current position.\n- *keydelay*, *keyhold*, *typedelay*, *typehold* - Set the delay between/holding each key.\n\n## Repository\n\nThe official repository is hosted on SourceHut at git.sr.ht/~geb/dotool, with mirrors available on GitHub."],"durationSeconds":7.358228082999587,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f435911b-6667-47dc-8ba0-0334c9812e20","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"4068f2b6-124b-408f-9e89-4a57d868b659","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eb024d06-ea54-4013-8a4b-fece8f91b5e3","agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012yi1Jfh2nCJxi6FcMw2Mqd","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"SendInput blocked elevated window UAC AutoHotkey macOS accessibility permission CGEvent automation restrictions\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Windows Vista/7 UAC is blocking Send keystrokes :\\\\ Help! - Ask for Help - AutoHotkey Community\",\"url\":\"https://www.autohotkey.com/board/topic/69774-windows-vista7-uac-is-blocking-send-keystrokes-help/\"},{\"title\":\"Only elevate UIAccess applications | CalCom\",\"url\":\"https://calcomsoftware.com/elevate-uiaccess-apps-installed-in-secure-locations/\"},{\"title\":\"Sending Keystrokes to Elevated CMD Window - Ask for Help - AutoHotkey Community\",\"url\":\"https://www.autohotkey.com/board/topic/47334-sending-keystrokes-to-elevated-cmd-window/\"},{\"title\":\"Elevated Permissions in AutoHotkey and the Run command - Ask for Help - AutoHotkey Community\",\"url\":\"https://www.autohotkey.com/board/topic/38434-elevated-permissions-in-autohotkey-and-the-run-command/\"},{\"title\":\"Autohotkey_L cannot send commands to Windows 8 windows? - Ask for Help - AutoHotkey Community\",\"url\":\"https://www.autohotkey.com/board/topic/84513-autohotkey-l-cannot-send-commands-to-windows-8-windows/\"},{\"title\":\"Implementing Auto-Type on macOS - Igor Kulman\",\"url\":\"https://blog.kulman.sk/implementing-auto-type-on-macos/\"},{\"title\":\"Accessibility Permission In Sandbox For Keyboard\",\"url\":\"https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/789896\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here's what I found regarding SendInput, UAC, AutoHotkey, and macOS automation restrictions:\n\n## Windows/AutoHotkey UAC Restrictions\n\nAutoHotkey scripts need to run as admin to be able to interact with any windows requiring admin privileges (e.g. the UAC window, Control Panel, etc.). For an AutoHotkey script to interact with any elevated application it has to itself be launched as elevated.\n\nSend and SendInput have no issues with UAC being on when used with an elevated command interpreter, though SendInput function is used to drive any application window, which requires appropriate permissions.\n\n## macOS Accessibility Permissions\n\nOn macOS, the automation restrictions are different. The system prompt to grant Accessibility permission can only be shown once per app launch. If the user dismisses the prompt or does not grant permission, your app must be restarted to show the prompt again. Users should enable the application in System Settings → Privacy & Security → Accessibility.\n\nAn app must be trusted for Accessibility to send events to other apps. For keyboard automation using CGEvent on macOS, users need to grant the app the System Settings > Privacy & Security > Input Monitoring privilege.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"2d4130ee-835c-4979-b7a5-60e264ab5fb9","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:42:37.215Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"SendInput blocked elevated window UAC AutoHotkey macOS accessibility permission CGEvent automation restrictions","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_0172VWHYGNURyJB7QRxFvo1M","content":[{"title":"Windows Vista/7 UAC is blocking Send keystrokes :\\ Help! - Ask for Help - AutoHotkey Community","url":"https://www.autohotkey.com/board/topic/69774-windows-vista7-uac-is-blocking-send-keystrokes-help/"},{"title":"Only elevate UIAccess applications | CalCom","url":"https://calcomsoftware.com/elevate-uiaccess-apps-installed-in-secure-locations/"},{"title":"Sending Keystrokes to Elevated CMD Window - Ask for Help - AutoHotkey Community","url":"https://www.autohotkey.com/board/topic/47334-sending-keystrokes-to-elevated-cmd-window/"},{"title":"Elevated Permissions in AutoHotkey and the Run command - Ask for Help - AutoHotkey Community","url":"https://www.autohotkey.com/board/topic/38434-elevated-permissions-in-autohotkey-and-the-run-command/"},{"title":"Autohotkey_L cannot send commands to Windows 8 windows? - Ask for Help - AutoHotkey Community","url":"https://www.autohotkey.com/board/topic/84513-autohotkey-l-cannot-send-commands-to-windows-8-windows/"},{"title":"Implementing Auto-Type on macOS - Igor Kulman","url":"https://blog.kulman.sk/implementing-auto-type-on-macos/"},{"title":"Accessibility Permission In Sandbox For Keyboard","url":"https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/789896"}]},"Based on the search results, here's what I found regarding SendInput, UAC, AutoHotkey, and macOS automation restrictions:\n\n## Windows/AutoHotkey UAC Restrictions\n\nAutoHotkey scripts need to run as admin to be able to interact with any windows requiring admin privileges (e.g. the UAC window, Control Panel, etc.). For an AutoHotkey script to interact with any elevated application it has to itself be launched as elevated.\n\nSend and SendInput have no issues with UAC being on when used with an elevated command interpreter, though SendInput function is used to drive any application window, which requires appropriate permissions.\n\n## macOS Accessibility Permissions\n\nOn macOS, the automation restrictions are different. The system prompt to grant Accessibility permission can only be shown once per app launch. If the user dismisses the prompt or does not grant permission, your app must be restarted to show the prompt again. Users should enable the application in System Settings → Privacy & Security → Accessibility.\n\nAn app must be trusted for Accessibility to send events to other apps. For keyboard automation using CGEvent on macOS, users need to grant the app the System Settings > Privacy & Security > Input Monitoring privilege."],"durationSeconds":6.721191124999896,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4068f2b6-124b-408f-9e89-4a57d868b659","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}

For enterprise deployments, unattended robots cost $8,000 to $15,000 per robot per year depending on capabilities.\n\n## Cost Complaints\n\nThere are documented complaints about UiPath pricing being expensive:\n\n- Reviewers describe pricing as mixed: some call it cost-effective and flexible, while others say enterprise licensing is expensive and some features or components cost extra.\n\n- UiPath is slightly more expensive than Automation Anywhere and Microsoft Power Automate.\n\n- UiPath has at least 6 documented hidden costs that can significantly increase total cost of ownership.\n\n- The pricing structure creates a cost structure that escalates sharply as robot counts grow — routinely contrasted against flatter-priced workflow and iPaaS alternatives, and AI features, Process Mining, Document Understanding, and orchestration add-ons layer consumption charges on top of base robot licenses.\n\n## Comparison with Automation Anywhere\n\n\"The cost of deployment is very high in Automation Anywhere whereas there is entry-level pricing in UiPath.\" However, Automation Anywhere's prices can range based on bot usage and number of processes automated, with entry-level plans starting at around $300/month per bot.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown There's discussion about creating a Wayland backend, though it's unclear if anyone is actively working on it.\n\nThe library appears to rely on Xwayland for partial Wayland support rather than having native Wayland backend implementations, which limits its functionality on pure Wayland environments.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"1e4ab630-e253-4ab2-be82-3f1cdaacb30a","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:42:59.535Z","toolUseResult":{"query":"pynput keyboard python library maintenance 2025 wayland root issues","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01X4h82fwKgSEXMAGkaMAeYX","content":[{"title":"Wrong keyboard layout while typing in wayland · Issue #639 · moses-palmer/pynput","url":"https://github.com/moses-palmer/pynput/issues/639"},{"title":"pynput keyboard listener doesnt work on wayland (even with uinput) · Issue #628 · moses-palmer/pynput","url":"https://github.com/moses-palmer/pynput/issues/628"},{"title":"Ubuntu Manpage: pynput - pynput Documentation This library allows you to control and monitor input devices. It contains subpackages for each type of input device supported:","url":"https://manpages.ubuntu.com/manpages/noble/man3/pynput.3.html"},{"title":"Platform limitations — pynput 1.7.6 documentation","url":"https://pynput.readthedocs.io/en/latest/limitations.html"},{"title":"Wayland (protocol)","url":"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayland_(protocol)"},{"title":"Wayland backend · Issue #331 · moses-palmer/pynput","url":"https://github.com/moses-palmer/pynput/issues/331"},{"title":"Top Python Libraries","url":"https://pythonlibraries.substack.com/p/pynput-cross-platform-mouse-and-keyboard"},{"title":"pynput cross platform mouse and keyboard automation with python 50c6602fd65d","url":"https://medium.com/top-python-libraries/pynput-cross-platform-mouse-and-keyboard-automation-with-python-50c6602fd65d"},{"title":"pypi.org","url":"https://pypi.org/project/wayremap"}]},"Here are the search results for pynput keyboard Python library maintenance, Wayland, and root issues:\n\n## Key Issues Found:\n\n**Wayland Compatibility Issues:**\n- pynput keyboard listener doesn't work on wayland, even with uinput, and users have tried running with root without success\n- When using keyboard.type() with a FR keyboard layout enabled on Ubuntu 24.04 LTS using wayland, it types as if it was a US keyboard, causing gibberish output\n- When running under Wayland, the X server emulator Xwayland provides limited functionality, and you will only receive input events from applications running under this emulator\n\n**Root/Privileges Requirements:**\n- The process must run as root (for macOS keyboard monitoring, though this indicates a pattern for privileged input access)\n- For Wayland security model, keyboard remapping tools must run with root permission\n\n**Current State:**\nThe search results show active GitHub issues from early 2025 (February-March 2025) indicating ongoing problems with Wayland support in pynput. There's discussion about creating a Wayland backend, though it's unclear if anyone is actively working on it.\n\nThe library appears to rely on Xwayland for partial Wayland support rather than having native Wayland backend implementations, which limits its functionality on pure Wayland environments."],"durationSeconds":7.815590458000079,"searchCount":1},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a8a6fdb7-4735-4906-a977-26e64b997e31","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}
