{"parentUuid":"22003bdf-7e1f-4b97-bcfe-e9090d9a3a75","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aef3a266b8c58f5fd","message":{"model":"claude-fable-5","id":"msg_011CdVVy69v61eRmkZSGYTic","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"# Market research: declarative desktop-action executors (2025–2026)\n\n## 1. Tool-by-tool landscape\n\n| Tool | Platforms | Status (mid-2026) | License / price | Biggest complaints |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| [AutoHotkey v2](https://www.autohotkey.com/docs/v2/ChangeLog.htm) | Windows only | Active (v2 GA Dec 2022, ongoing point releases; [KeySharp](https://textexpander.com/blog/autohotkey-alternatives) .NET cross-platform rewrite still alpha) | GPLv2, free | Windows-only forever; idiosyncratic language; v1→v2 migration split the community; [can't touch elevated windows unless the script is itself elevated](https://www.autohotkey.com/board/topic/69774-windows-vista7-uac-is-blocking-send-keystrokes-help/) |\n| [Hammerspoon](https://www.hammerspoon.org/) | macOS | Maintained, free OSS | MIT | Lua config, not a CLI; breaks when Apple moves APIs; TCC permission dance |\n| [Keyboard Maestro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keyboard_Maestro) | macOS | Active commercial (11.0.4, May 2025) | ~$36 | GUI-first, closed source, macros not versionable text files |\n| [cliclick](https://github.com/BlueM/cliclick) | macOS | Alive but sleepy (v5.x; releases years apart) | BSD, free | Primitives only — no files, no waits, no verification, no screenshots |\n| [xdotool](https://github.com/jordansissel/xdotool) | Linux/X11 only | Semi-revived — dormant ~2021–2025, then [v4.20260303.1 (Mar 2026)](https://tracker.debian.org/pkg/xdotool) | BSD | X11 only; [under XWayland it only reaches XWayland clients](https://github.com/jordansissel/xdotool/releases/tag/v3.20210903.1); modifier-key leakage bugs |\n| [ydotool](https://github.com/ReimuNotMoe/ydotool) | Linux (uinput → X11, Wayland, TTY) | Maintained-ish, community | AGPLv3 | Needs `ydotoold` daemon + root/udev on `/dev/uinput`; [autostart/permissions are a recurring headache](https://medium.com/@Spritan/how-to-auto-start-ydotool-on-linux-wayland-36a39323c2c0); [non-ASCII/layout failures](https://bbs.archlinux.org/viewtopic.php?id=284968); no window ops, no absolute-position sanity on multi-monitor |\n| [dotool](https://git.sr.ht/~geb/dotool) | Linux (uinput; X11/Wayland/TTY) | Maintained, small (Go, GPLv3) | GPLv3 | stdin command stream, no daemon — but same uinput permission model; percent-based `mouseto` only; no verification |\n| [wtype](https://alternativeto.net/software/wtype/) / wlrctl | Wayland (wlroots protocols) | Niche | MIT | Keyboard only (wtype); doesn't work on GNOME/KDE (virtual-keyboard protocol unsupported); no window targeting |\n| [PyAutoGUI](https://github.com/asweigart/pyautogui/issues/747) | Win/mac/X11 | **Effectively unmaintained**; [zero Wayland support, open since 2016](https://github.com/asweigart/pyautogui/issues/111), screenshots come back black on Wayland | BSD | Wayland dead-end; slow; `locateOnScreen` flaky; needs Python env; abandoned issue tracker |\n| [pynput](https://pynput.readthedocs.io/en/latest/limitations.html) | Win/mac/X11 | Maintained-ish | LGPL | [Wayland listener broken even with uinput](https://github.com/moses-palmer/pynput/issues/628), [wrong layouts under Wayland](https://github.com/moses-palmer/pynput/issues/639); macOS needs root for monitoring |\n| [robotjs](https://github.com/hurdlegroup/robotjs) | Win/mac/X11 | **Original dead** (won't even install on modern npm); community forks ([@jitsi/robotjs](https://github.com/jitsi/robotjs), @hurdlegroup) keep prebuilds alive | MIT | Native-module build pain in CI; no Wayland; API frozen in 2018 |\n| [nut.js](https://nutjs.dev/blog/i-give-up) | Win/mac/X11 | **Went proprietary-subscription May 2024** (\"I'm giving up on open source\"); [repo public but prebuilt packages are paywalled](https://github.com/nut-tree/nut.js), updates land for subscribers first; community runs [@nut-tree-fork](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@nut-tree-fork/nut-js) (last publish ~a year ago) | Proprietary sub / build-from-source | The rug-pull itself is the complaint; fork is stale; no Wayland |\n| [SikuliX → OculiX](https://docs.oculix.org/) | Win/mac/Linux (Java) | RaiMan stopped 2025, [archived upstream and handed stewardship to OculiX in March 2026](https://raimans-sikulix.gitbook.io/sikulix.com); [OculiX v3.0.3 stable, v4 in flight, OpenCV 4.10 / Java 17+](https://github.com/oculix-org/Oculix) | MIT, free | JVM + IDE heavyweight; image-only targeting; scripts rot with theme/DPI changes; decade of \"is it dead?\" uncertainty |\n| Selenium / Playwright | Browser (+ [experimental Electron via CDP](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/automated-testing)) | Very active | Apache/MIT | Hard boundary: browser/DOM only. Native dialogs, file pickers, other apps = out of scope. This boundary is exactly where desktop tools get cobbled in |\n| [Power Automate Desktop](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/power-automate/desktop-flows/repair-selector) | Windows | Active; Microsoft added [selector self-repair](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/power-automate/desktop-flows/repair-selector) and [AI self-healing (preview)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/power-platform/release-plan/2024wave2/power-automate/self-heal-ui-browser-automation-actions-at-execution-ai) — an admission selectors flake | Free attended w/ Win 10/11; premium for unattended/cloud | Sluggish, org-account/cloud-flow storage coupling, flows aren't reviewable text, selector fragility |\n| [UiPath](https://costbench.com/software/rpa/uipath/) / Automation Anywhere | Windows-centric | Active enterprise | [$420–$1,930/mo plans; unattended robots $8k–15k/robot/yr](https://vendorbenchmark.com/blog/uipath-vs-automation-anywhere-pricing-benchmark) | Cost escalation, heavyweight studios, licensing complexity; notable: UiPath ships a whole [Computer Vision stack specifically because selectors die on Citrix/VDI/streamed pixels](https://www.uipath.com/platform/agentic-automation/ai-ecosystem/citrix-automation) |\n| [TagUI](https://github.com/aisingapore/TagUI) (closest historical analog: free cross-platform CLI running declarative flow files) | Win/mac/Linux | **AI Singapore discontinued maintenance**; community-only, flagged [inactive](https://snyk.io/advisor/python/tagui) | Apache-2.0 | Bundles PHP+Chrome+SikuliX Java — anything but a single binary; X11-era Linux support |\n\nNewer entrants worth knowing: [enigo](https://github.com/enigo-rs/enigo) (Rust cross-platform input crate, MIT, active, 1.8k★ — X11 solid, **Wayland/libei experimental with known bugs and US-layout-only caveats**; used/forked by RustDesk), [espanso](https://espanso.org/docs/install/linux/) (Rust, proves cross-platform injection incl. Wayland EVDEV is shippable — at the cost of `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` and \"experimental\" Wayland status), [Botwork](https://github.com/dhilipsiva/botwork) (Rust single-binary Robot-Framework alternative — stalled at 0.2, ~2023, validates the itch but not the market).\n\n## 2. The gap\n\n**Direct answer: no maintained, cross-platform, single-binary CLI that executes desktop input from declarative files with built-in verification exists as of mid-2026.** Everything is missing at least two legs:\n\n- **[terminator (mediar-ai)](https://github.com/mediar-ai/terminator)** — the closest thing in spirit: Rust core, MIT, CLI + YAML workflows, \"deterministic workflows, AI only for recovery, >95% success,\" $2.8M raised, active (1.6k★). But **Windows-only** and accessibility-tree-based, not coordinate-based.\n- **[agent-desktop](https://github.com/lahfir/agent-desktop)** — Rust single binary, structured JSON, exit codes, deterministic element refs. **macOS-only** (Win/Linux \"planned\"), a11y-tree-based, agent-oriented rather than declarative-file-oriented.\n- **[Maestro](https://maestro.dev/)** — proves the declarative-YAML-flow UX is loved (`tapOn`, `assertVisible`, auto-wait/retry) — but mobile/web only.\n- **[openQA/os-autoinst](https://open.qa/docs/)** — proves the *exact* pixelcoords/pixelactions data model at industrial scale: SUSE/openSUSE QA runs on \"needles\" = [PNG screenshot + JSON of labeled areas with match thresholds and click points](https://github.com/os-autoinst/os-autoinst-needles-openQA), fuzzy template matching, assertion-driven. But it's a Perl monolith welded to QEMU/VNC for OS-image testing, not a desktop CLI.\n\nWhat people actually cobble: platform-conditional shell (`xdotool` on X11, `ydotool`/`dotool` + udev rules on Wayland, `cliclick`/AppleScript on macOS, AHK/PowerShell on Windows) glued with sleeps and prayer. Evidence of the seam: [SeleniumBase users asking for Wayland fallbacks to PyAutoGUI actions](https://github.com/seleniumbase/SeleniumBase/issues/4010), [touchegg debating ydotool vs dotool](https://github.com/JoseExposito/touchegg/issues/656), [AI tools shipping \"X11 only\" caveats](https://github.com/DonTizi/ReMind/issues/6), and a cottage industry of \"PyAutoGUI alternatives\" listicles ([TestDriver's](https://testdriver.ai/articles/top-12-alternatives-to-pyautogui-for-windows-macos-linux-testing) recommend... browser tools and Appium — i.e., nothing actually fills it). On Wayland, the real 2025–2026 development is plumbing-level: [libei + the XDG RemoteDesktop/InputCapture portals](http://who-t.blogspot.com/2026/07/libei-integrations-in-xdg-remotedesktop.html), with XTEST→libei translation in XWayland — portal-mediated, permission-prompted injection is becoming the sanctioned path, and almost no end-user tool has wrapped it well yet.\n\n## 3. Computer-use agents as a market force\n\nHow they execute today:\n- **Anthropic computer use**: the [reference quickstart](https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-quickstarts/blob/main/computer-use-demo/Dockerfile) is an Ubuntu/Xvfb Docker container where the ComputerTool [shells out to xdotool for clicks and scrot for screenshots](https://simonwillison.net/2024/Oct/22/computer-use/); the model emits pixel coordinates from screenshots.\n- **OpenAI Operator → ChatGPT agent**: [CUA model emits click/type/scroll actions against screenshots; \"your harness executes those actions\"](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/tools-computer-use) — OpenAI runs its own [cloud virtual browser/computer](https://openai.com/index/introducing-chatgpt-agent/); the API pushes execution onto you.\n- **Open-source/research**: [OSWorld standardized on raw `pyautogui` as the action space](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.07972), so UI-TARS, Agent S2 and most academic agents literally emit pyautogui snippets — inheriting all its platform holes. Sandbox vendors ([E2B desktop](https://e2b.dev/docs/use-cases/computer-use), [trycua/cua](https://github.com/trycua/cua) — YC-backed, MIT, cross-OS sandboxes + MCP server, [Bytebot](https://github.com/topics/cua)) each ship their own injector inside a VM/container.\n- **MCP desktop-control servers are proliferating and thin**: [computer-control-mcp (PyAutoGUI+OCR)](https://github.com/AB498/computer-control-mcp), [MCPControl (Windows)](https://github.com/claude-did-this/MCPControl), [mcp-desktop-automation (robotjs, 10MB screenshot limits)](https://glama.ai/mcp/servers/@tanob/mcp-desktop-automation), [a Rust computer-use MCP for Win/mac](https://github.com/zavora-ai/computer-use-mcp), [Windows-MCP](https://mcp.directory/blog/windows-mcp-guide). Mostly wrappers over the same broken substrates — none offer verified, replayable execution.\n\nDemand signal for a deterministic executor is real and articulated: terminator's whole pitch is *\"pre-train workflows as deterministic code, call AI only when recovery is needed\"*; [Microsoft Copilot Studio computer use](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-copilot/blog/copilot-studio/computer-using-agents-now-deliver-more-secure-ui-automation-at-scale/) sells auditability; research keeps documenting that [CUAs misclick and misground on professional UIs](https://arxiv.org/html/2604.17849v1), spawning [pre-execution click-verification guardrails](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.14707) and [\"deterministic replay\" as a missing primitive](https://www.sakurasky.com/blog/missing-primitives-for-trustworthy-ai-part-8/). An agent that *plans once* and then replays a pixelcoords-verified flow — with point-asserts gating each step and exit codes on drift — is exactly the \"LLM for flexibility, deterministic execution for correctness\" architecture practitioners are converging on.\n\n## 4. Adjacent validation (GUI test automation)\n\n- **[WinAppDriver is dead](https://testguild.com/automation-tools-desktop/)** — no release since Nov 2020, 1,100+ open issues, closed-source server, obsolete JSON Wire protocol; [Appium's Windows driver is hostage to it](https://github.com/appium/appium-windows-driver), with [FlaUI (MIT, v5.0.0 Feb 2025)](https://github.com/FlaUI/FlaUI.WebDriver) and NovaWindows as the .NET escape hatches. Windows desktop test automation is in a vacuum Microsoft declined to fill.\n- **Linux a11y route**: [dogtail still ships (2.0 line, Oct 2025)](https://pypi.org/project/dogtail/2.0.dev2/) and does Wayland only via [gnome-ponytail-daemon working around \"Wayland does not expose desktop coordinates\"](https://modehnal.github.io/) with the RemoteDesktop/ScreenCast APIs — GNOME-shaped, and heavy AT-SPI use [can segfault gnome-shell](http://www.mail-archive.com/desktop-bugs@lists.ubuntu.com/msg833183.html).\n- **Where coordinates still beat a11y trees** (the durable niche): [Citrix/VDI/RDP where the app is literally a streamed picture](https://www.accelirate.com/challenges-of-rpa-in-citrix-environment/) — the reason [UiPath built Computer Vision and leads Gartner on Citrix automation](https://medium.com/@praveenb0927/uipath-computer-vision-cv-626387166168); games/canvas/custom-drawn widgets with no a11y layer; [legacy apps without accessibility implementations](https://docs.uipath.com/activities/other/latest/ui-automation/known-issues-and-limitations-citrix); cross-app OS-level flows browsers can't reach; Electron/webview apps where the a11y tree is partial or must be force-enabled. Also note [tauri-driver is still \"pre-alpha\"](https://v2.tauri.app/develop/tests/) and Playwright's Electron support is experimental — even the modern desktop-app stacks lack solid E2E paths, which is why [TestDriver.ai ($20/mo+, vision-based)](https://testdriver.ai/pricing) and [AskUI ($50–600/mo)](https://checkthat.ai/brands/askui) exist as venture-backed commercial answers.\n\n## 5. Positioning white space — and honest weaknesses\n\n**What would make pixelactions legitimately more than \"just use pyautogui\":**\n1. **Human-verified ground truth + self-healing + asserts is the openQA needle model, portable.** Nobody ships it as a desktop CLI. pyautogui's `locateOnScreen` is the degenerate version (no labels, no provenance, no exit-code contract). The pixelcoords→pixelactions pipeline is \"needles for any desktop,\" reviewable in git.\n2. **Wayland is genuinely unserved.** PyAutoGUI never will; pynput can't; xdotool can't; ydotool/dotool are Linux-only primitives with uinput permission friction and no verification. A tool that wraps uinput *and* the libei/portal path cleanly, with documented setup, owns a growing audience (Wayland is now the default on GNOME/KDE distros).\n3. **Agent guardrail/tool niche is nascent and sloppy.** MCP desktop servers are pyautogui/robotjs wrappers. A deterministic executor with pre/post point-verification, drift detection (template re-location), and machine-readable failure is a better tool for agents to call — \"agent plans, pixelactions replays with receipts.\"\n4. **CI for desktop apps + kiosk/QA rigs**: WinAppDriver's death, tauri-driver's pre-alpha state, and streamed-pixel environments (Citrix, VMs, VNC-only rigs) all reward a coordinate+template executor with exit codes that slots into CI like any other CLI.\n\n**Where the idea is weak — don't kid yourself:**\n- **The industry's center of gravity is a11y-first.** Both credible new entrants (terminator, agent-desktop) chose accessibility trees *for* determinism and explicitly avoided coordinates; Power Automate and UiPath treat vision as fallback. Coordinates+templates must be positioned as the layer for where trees don't exist — not as the general answer — or the pitch reads as regression.\n- **\"Single binary, no setup\" is not fully honest on any platform.** Wayland needs uinput udev/capabilities or portal consent prompts; macOS needs Accessibility/Input-Monitoring TCC grants ([once-per-launch prompt quirks included](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/789896)); Windows can't inject into elevated windows from a non-elevated process. Espanso and enigo show it's tractable and show the scar tissue ([Wayland \"experimental,\" layout bugs](https://github.com/espanso/espanso/issues/2162), [libei \"currently some bugs\"](https://github.com/enigo-rs/enigo)). Budget serious per-platform engineering; enigo is a starting point, not a solution.\n- **Coordinate spaces are a swamp** — Windows physical px vs macOS points vs Wayland refusing global coordinates entirely (dotool resorts to percentages; dogtail needed a daemon). Your pixelcoords `CoordSpace` work is the moat here, and it has to be.\n- **Solo-maintainer economics in this exact niche are documented misery**: nut.js gave up, TagUI's sponsor walked, xdotool went quiet for four years, SikuliX outlived its maintainer's patience. The counterweight is that computer-use money changed the climate (terminator $2.8M, cua in YC, TestDriver commercial) — but that money is flowing to *agent-native* tools, so the MCP/agent-tool framing is likely the commercially relevant one, with CI/Wayland users as the organic base.\n\nBottom line: the specific combination — cross-platform incl. Wayland, single binary, declarative files, human-verified coordinate ground truth, template self-healing, assert-gated execution with exit codes — exists nowhere today; its nearest neighbors are Windows-only (terminator), macOS-only (agent-desktop), mobile-only (Maestro), or VM-QA-only (openQA). The gap is real; the risk is not competition but substrate pain (Wayland/macOS/Windows injection) and the a11y-first tide, which the pixelcoords verification story is the right answer to — provided it's positioned as \"deterministic execution where trees fail, receipts everywhere.\""}],"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":400,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3878,"cache_read_input_tokens":83269,"output_tokens":9345,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3878},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":400,"output_tokens":9345,"cache_read_input_tokens":83269,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3878,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3878,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdVVy45eZEvy7CLyBj7aN","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"beed1cf0-11cc-4787-b775-b7c350653914","timestamp":"2026-07-29T01:45:14.742Z","effort":"xhigh","sessionKind":"bg","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/dev/dev/pixelcoords","sessionId":"ed39502a-f56f-4b6d-9e00-f726faad43da","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main"}
